sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="settings_select_dashboard-1" />

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Add widgets in each section</span>*

With the **section scope** set, you can add **widgets**.

Each **section scope** type has different **widgets** available for
**visualizations**. Click on the <img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=22&amp;name=29.png"
style="width: 22px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=11&amp;name=29.png 11w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=22&amp;name=29.png 22w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=33&amp;name=29.png 33w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=44&amp;name=29.png 44w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=55&amp;name=29.png 55w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/29.png?width=66&amp;name=29.png 66w"
sizes="(max-width: 22px) 100vw, 22px" width="22" alt="29" />button at
the bottom of each new **section** to select **widgets** for that
section (you can add multiple **widgets** per section).

These are the **widgets** available for **fleet scope sections**:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=600&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=300&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=600&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=900&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=1200&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=1500&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_widgets-1.png?width=1800&amp;name=fleet_widgets-1.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="fleet_widgets-1" />

These are the **widgets** available for **robot scope sections**:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_widgets.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=300&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=900&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=1200&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=1500&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_widgets.png?width=1800&amp;name=robot_widgets.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="robot_widgets" />

There is currently a single special-purpose widget for **navigation
sections**:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=700&amp;name=navigation_widget.png"
style="width: 700px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=350&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=700&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=1050&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=1400&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=1750&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/navigation_widget.png?width=2100&amp;name=navigation_widget.png 2100w"
sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700"
alt="navigation_widget" />

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">What are the control widgets?</span>*

There are 2 control **widgets** available: **Fleet Control** and **Robot
Control**. These should definitely be added to any new dashboards where
you want to be able to select within the fleet or focus on a particular
robot.

**Fleet Control** lets you filter your robot fleet by a collection (tag)
and/or an attribute status. It also allows grouping robots by a
**collection**, or sorting by status or alphabetical order.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=600&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=300&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=600&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=900&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=1200&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=1500&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/fleet_bar-1.png?width=1800&amp;name=fleet_bar-1.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="fleet_bar-1" />

**Robot Control** lets you search for a robot that you have access to
and set it as your selected robot to monitor. The widget also has handy
shortcuts, such as a button to restart the InOrbit **agent,** or another
that opens the **Navigation** dashboard.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_bar.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=300&amp;name=robot_bar.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_bar.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=900&amp;name=robot_bar.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=1200&amp;name=robot_bar.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=1500&amp;name=robot_bar.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Create%20and%20edit%20dashboards/robot_bar.png?width=1800&amp;name=robot_bar.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="robot_bar" />

 

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="button-container-id-camera-setup" class="hs-accordion__item"
aria-expanded="">

<span id="camera-setup" class="anchor"></span>

Setting up and adjusting camera images
<span class="hs-accordion__item-icon"></span>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<span style="color: #222222;">InOrbit’s agent can subscribe to ROS image
topics (with messages of type
</span>[Image](http://docs.ros.org/en/noetic/api/sensor_msgs/html/msg/Image.html)<span style="color: #222222;">
and
</span>[CompressedImage](https://docs.ros.org/en/api/sensor_msgs/html/msg/CompressedImage.html)<span style="color: #222222;">)
and send samples of those images to the cloud.
</span><span style="color: #222222;">The images are compressed as JPEG,
throttled and down-sampled. </span>

<span style="color: #222222;">This image snapshot based approach is
designed to provide the user enough information about the robot’s
environment when monitoring or operating it, while minimizing CPU and
network usage on the robot. There is a tradeoff between image quality,
size and frame rate, as we’ll see in the
</span>[configuration](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_iGLr_uKSW_chB8XL9EnoYahUHDn5wOwvcLUAr3v_4U/edit#heading=h.juk8wgb76m1p)<span style="color: #222222;">
section.</span>

<span style="color: #222222;">Video encoding and streaming is available
as a separate offering. Please contact
</span>[support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai)<span style="color: #222222;">
for more information.</span>

## <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #000000;">Configuration</span>

<span style="color: #222222;">If you go to Settings \> Navigation \>
Cameras, you’ll find a ‘Configuration preset’ dropdown with 3
options:</span>

<span style="color: #222222;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=600&amp;name=33.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=300&amp;name=33.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=600&amp;name=33.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=900&amp;name=33.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=1200&amp;name=33.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=1500&amp;name=33.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/33.png?width=1800&amp;name=33.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="33" /></span>

<span style="color: #222222;">Each preset represent a different
configuration for processing and transmitting camera images from the
agent to the cloud:</span>

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|   | **<span style="color: #222222;">Higher Frequency</span>** | **<span style="color: #222222;">Bandwidth Intensive</span>** | **<span style="color: #222222;">Higher Quality</span>** |
| <span style="color: #222222;">Encoding</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">mono</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">RGB</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">RGB</span> |
| [JPEG compression quality](https://docs.opencv.org/3.4/d8/d6a/group__imgcodecs__flags.html#ga292d81be8d76901bff7988d18d2b42ac) | <span style="color: #222222;">10</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">15</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">30</span> |
| <span style="color: #222222;">Size</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">380x240</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">380x240</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">720x380</span> |
| <span style="color: #222222;">Frame Rate \[Hz\]</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">2</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">1</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">0.2</span> |
| <span style="color: #222222;">Message size \[kB\]</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">3</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">4.5</span> | <span style="color: #222222;">12</span> |

 

<span style="color: #222222;">These are the combinations we offer in
terms of quality / size / frame rate balance, though we'd recommend
using 'Higher Frequency' to preserve bandwidth if you don't need a high
definition. With the default account set-up, if you make resolution and
frequency too high, you may start to experience lag (latency), which is
not a desired effect.</span>

## <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #000000;">Customizing camera views</span>

<span style="color: #000000;">If you need to focus on the images from a
given camera widget, you can enlarge it by clicking within it:</span>

<span style="color: #000000;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=300&amp;name=34.png"
style="width: 300px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=150&amp;name=34.png 150w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=300&amp;name=34.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=450&amp;name=34.png 450w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=600&amp;name=34.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=750&amp;name=34.png 750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/34.png?width=900&amp;name=34.png 900w"
sizes="(max-width: 300px) 100vw, 300px" width="300" alt="34" /></span>

<span style="color: #000000;">You can further customize the view of a
camera widget by clicking on the camera label (‘C’, ‘L’ or ‘R’ in the
example below). The label acts like a switch that allows you to
alternate between: </span>

- <span style="color: #000000;">zoom images</span>
- <span style="color: #000000;">turn off a camera</span>
- <span style="color: #000000;">full images</span>

<span style="color: #000000;">Turning cameras off is a useful way to
save resources during regular operations, when camera images are not
strictly needed.</span>

<span style="color: #000000;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=349&amp;name=36.png"
style="width: 349px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=175&amp;name=36.png 175w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=349&amp;name=36.png 349w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=524&amp;name=36.png 524w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=698&amp;name=36.png 698w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=873&amp;name=36.png 873w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/36.png?width=1047&amp;name=36.png 1047w"
sizes="(max-width: 349px) 100vw, 349px" width="349" alt="36" /></span>

<span style="color: #000000;">If you need to re-enable a camera for a
moment, then you can easily turn it back on by clicking on the
label.</span>

<span style="color: #000000;">Please note that, in order to preserve
bandwidth, InOrbit’s agent sends images to the cloud only if a user from
a company (at least one) has a Navigation Dashboard open and the camera
on. </span>

## <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #000000;">High-resolution mode</span>

<span style="color: #222222;">InOrbit offers an advanced option to
display the featured camera in an alternative resolution for  a limited
time (it currently times out after 1 minute), using the Hi-rez switch.
This enables you to deeply optimize bandwidth usage by only transmitting
full quality images when an active operator needs that quality to
discern a detail, such as an aisle or zone number. </span>

<span style="color: #222222;">The Hi-Rez mode provides high quality
images, achieving the goal of being able to see details for a brief
period of time. The update frequency is trimmed down to 0.1 Hz to
preserve bandwidth. Just click the Hi-rez switch to toggle it
on/off.</span>

<span style="font-size: 12px; color: #807575;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=624&amp;name=37.png"
style="width: 624px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=312&amp;name=37.png 312w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=624&amp;name=37.png 624w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=936&amp;name=37.png 936w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=1248&amp;name=37.png 1248w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=1560&amp;name=37.png 1560w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/37.png?width=1872&amp;name=37.png 1872w"
sizes="(max-width: 624px) 100vw, 624px" width="624" alt="37" /></span>

## <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #000000;">Advanced settings</span>

<span style="color: #222222;">Besides the default presets, it is
possible to select any arbitrary configuration that you prefer (for both
the regular image snapshots and Hi-rez mode). For that, you will need to
provide the following parameters to our </span>[Customer Success
team](mailto:success@inorbit.ai)<span style="color: #222222;">:</span>

- <span style="color: #222222;">Encoding (mono or rgb)</span>
- <span style="color: #222222;">JPEG compression quality</span>
- <span style="color: #222222;">Image size</span>
- <span style="color: #222222;">Frame rate \[Hz\]</span>

<span style="color: #222222;">After setting up that configuration,
cameras will start streaming with the desired resolution and frequency
that you choose, along with the preset options in the dropdown
menu.</span>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="button-container-id-teleoperation" class="hs-accordion__item"
aria-expanded="">

<span id="teleoperation" class="anchor"></span>

Navigation: Relocalization + Teleoperation
<span class="hs-accordion__item-icon"></span>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

When autonomy fails and other means of resolution fall short,
<a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a> offers powerful
Intervention capabilities that allow you to temporarily take manual
control of your robots and teleoperate them back to safety and autonomy.

This ability gives the human operator using InOrbit a greater power over
the physical presence of the robot so it must be used with caution.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Overview</span>

Relocalization and Teleoperation are available when opening the
Navigation screen for a particular robot. The controls for both
relocalization and teleoperation can be found on the right side of the
Navigation dashboard.

**<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=600&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=300&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=600&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=900&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=1200&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=1500&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/map_navigation_overview.png?width=1800&amp;name=map_navigation_overview.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="map_navigation_overview" />**

InOrbit Control offers flexible layouts to display more detail across
maps and cameras. 

The “map/camera” button, between the map selector and the settings
button,  toggles between views that feature the map and the primary
selected camera.. 

Cameras are ordered based on the order of cameras in **Settings \>
Navigation \> Camera**. You can select the primary camera by clicking on
the image within any camera view. Similarly, you can feature the map by
clicking on the small map view when a camera is featured. You can turn
off a camera (good for reducing bandwidth) by clicking on the label in
the upper right of any camera view.

This is an example of featuring the primary camera.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=600&amp;name=full_camera.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=300&amp;name=full_camera.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=600&amp;name=full_camera.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=900&amp;name=full_camera.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=1200&amp;name=full_camera.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=1500&amp;name=full_camera.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/full_camera.png?width=1800&amp;name=full_camera.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="full_camera" />

To improve the video quality, InOrbit offers an advanced option to
display the featured camera in high resolution for  a limited time (it
currently times out after 1 minute), using the Hi-rez switch. This
enables you to deeply optimize bandwidth usage by only transmitting full
quality images when an active operator needs that quality to discern a
detail, such as an aisle or zone number. 

Updates will be significantly slower while in Hi-Rez mode based on the
additional data transmission, but the primary goal is to be able to see
details for a brief period of time. Just click the Hi-rez switch to
toggle it on/off.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=600&amp;name=hi_res.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=300&amp;name=hi_res.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=600&amp;name=hi_res.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=900&amp;name=hi_res.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=1200&amp;name=hi_res.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=1500&amp;name=hi_res.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/hi_res.png?width=1800&amp;name=hi_res.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="hi_res" />

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Relocalization</span>

To ensure that the robot is properly localized to accurately track and
control the robot navigation, the map displays lidar dots reflecting how
the robot is currently recognizing obstacles. If the lidar dots are not
properly oriented with features on the map, navigation becomes more
unreliable.

Relocalization may be all that is required to restore proper navigation
by the robot, or it may be the first step before taking over navigation
via teleoperation.

First activate relocalization by clicking the Relocalize button.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=600&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=300&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=600&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=900&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=1200&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=1500&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/navigation_map_reloc.png?width=1800&amp;name=navigation_map_reloc.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="navigation_map_reloc" />

It is easy to relocalize a robot with two controls:

- Clicking and dragging the rotation control above the robot avatar
  enables rotating the map features  
    
  <img
  src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=600&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png"
  style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
  srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=300&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=600&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=900&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=1200&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=1500&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_rotate.png?width=1800&amp;name=relocalize_rotate.png 1800w"
  sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
  alt="relocalize_rotate" />
- Clicking and dragging on the robot avatar enables translating the map
  in xy space to align the lidar dots with the features  
    
  <img
  src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=600&amp;name=relocalize_move.png"
  style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
  srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=300&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=600&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=900&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=1200&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=1500&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_move.png?width=1800&amp;name=relocalize_move.png 1800w"
  sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
  alt="relocalize_move" />

Click on the check mark in the lower right to confirm relocalization. If
you decide to cancel or start over, click the Relocalize button again to
abort the ongoing action.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=400&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png"
style="width: 400px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=200&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=400&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=600&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=800&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 800w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=1000&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 1000w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/relocalize_confirm.png?width=1200&amp;name=relocalize_confirm.png 1200w"
sizes="(max-width: 400px) 100vw, 400px" width="400"
alt="relocalize_confirm" />

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #666666;">Teleoperation</span>

<span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=600&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=300&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=600&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=900&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=1200&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=1500&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/map_navigation_teleop.png?width=1800&amp;name=map_navigation_teleop.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="map_navigation_teleop" /></span>

To provide flexible options for different needs/preferences, there are
currently two  modes available for Teleoperation:

- **Open** - send a very simple velocity or movement command to the
  robot to move in a certain way without invoking the robot’s navigation
  stack
- **Waypoint** - send a command to the robot to move to a specific
  target location, using its internal navigation stack

See the **Security measures** section below for important information
about how InOrbit restricts teleoperation to ensure the safest possible
operating conditions.

###### *<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;">Open Teleop</span>*

<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #000000;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=600&amp;name=teleop_active.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=300&amp;name=teleop_active.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=600&amp;name=teleop_active.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=900&amp;name=teleop_active.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=1200&amp;name=teleop_active.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=1500&amp;name=teleop_active.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/teleop_active.png?width=1800&amp;name=teleop_active.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="teleop_active" /></span>

Selecting Open Teleop activates a d-pad with arrow buttons in the lower
right corner. Clicking on the up or down arrows send commands to the
robot to move a single step forward or backward, relative to the current
robot position and orientation, respectively. Clicking on the right and
left arrows send commands to the robot to rotate clockwise or
counterclockwise by a fixed angle, respectively, again relative to the
robot.

Above the arrows are meters to show the linear velocity and angular
velocity when the robot is moving, and a network gauge to ensure that
the roundtrip latency is sufficiently low to safely carry out the
command.

Since <span style="text-decoration: underline;">this can be a risky mode
to be in</span> (as it bypasses the navigation stack that avoids
obstacles), the d-pad highlights in orange to make it very clear when
the robot is in Open Teleop mode. You can deactivate Open Teleop by
toggling off the Open Teleop button.

To set a new distance or angle of the increment for each step:

1.  Starting from the menu of three vertical dots at upper right, select
    **Settings \> Navigation \> Teleop**.
2.  *Optional* - Select whether this change should apply to the Fleet or
    an individual robot with the selector in the upper left.
3.  Adjust the default Distance or Angle using the sliders.
4.  Exit **Settings** and select the **Navigation** dashboard to resume
    Teleop.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=600&amp;name=38.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=300&amp;name=38.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=600&amp;name=38.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=900&amp;name=38.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=1200&amp;name=38.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=1500&amp;name=38.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/38.png?width=1800&amp;name=38.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="38" />

###### *<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;">Waypoint Teleop</span>*

###### <span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=600&amp;name=waypoint_active.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=300&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=600&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=900&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=1200&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=1500&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/waypoint_active.png?width=1800&amp;name=waypoint_active.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="waypoint_active" /></span>

Selecting Waypoint Teleop enables selecting a target pose that will send
a command to the robot to which it should move. Select the target
position by clicking on an arbitrary point on the map or by dragging the
pin icon that appears on the current robot avatar to the new target
position. Refine the target pose by clicking within the circle
indicating the target and dragging to orient the robot avatar to the
desired final orientation.

Click the check mark at the bottom to send the command for the robot to
move to the waypoint using its navigation stack. Once the robot’s
navigation stack computes the path, it will be displayed and the robot
will navigate to the target. The gauges on the right display the
velocity, network rate and angular velocity. 

You can abandon setting a waypoint by toggling off the Open Teleop
button.

If you have already triggered a waypoint navigation, InOrbit offers an
optional feature to cancel the navigation goal in progress. By clicking
on the “Cancel navigation goal” button you’ll be able to forcibly stop
the interaction while the robot is moving. Please contact
[support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai) if you would like this
activated on your account.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=60&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png"
style="width: 60px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=30&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 30w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=60&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 60w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=90&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 90w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=120&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 120w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=150&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 150w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Navigation%20Relocalization%20+%20Teleoperation/cancel_nav_goal.png?width=180&amp;name=cancel_nav_goal.png 180w"
sizes="(max-width: 60px) 100vw, 60px" width="60"
alt="cancel_nav_goal" />

###### *<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;">Step-by-step mode</span>*

Open Teleop has  two options,  **step-by-step** and  **continuous**
mode, which respectively only issue a single command at a time or allow
holding down a control to send a series of commands.

The default and recommended mode of operation gives you the ability to
“nudge” the robot one step forward, one step backwards or rotating a
fraction of a spin to the left or right.

This ability is intended to allow you to use discrete, safe movements to
move the robot past an obstacle that its autonomous navigation algorithm
can’t resolve on its own.

This enables a basic level of human intervention to apply in adverse
network conditions to support re-establishing autonomous operations
without the need for physical assistance.

###### *<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;">Continuous mode (Experimental)</span>*

InOrbit includes an experimental feature to allow continuous
teleoperation, similar to that found on remote control tools.

In order to enable continuous teleoperation mode:

1.  As Administrator, login to <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>.
2.  Starting from the menu of three vertical dots at upper right, select
    **Settings \> Navigation \> Teleop**.
3.  Turn the Step-by-Step mode switch off.

After this is done, the following functions become available:

- You can click on a direction arrow repeatedly to achieve a seemingly
  smooth movement. NOTE: the robot will move an entire foot in the given
  direction after the last click is pressed.
- You can switch to use a virtual joystick by clicking on an icon that
  appears in the bottom right of the teleop control.

<span style="text-decoration: underline;">For Open Teleop, we highly
recommend step-by-step mode</span> for safety reasons since the
navigation stack is not involved to prevent collisions with obstacles.

For Precision Teleop, the continuous mode only affects the process of
specifying the action by, for example, holding down the arrow to change
the distance in fixed increments before sending the final command, so it
is a convenience rather than a risk.

###### ***<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666;">Security measures</span>***

As teleoperation is a dangerous operation, InOrbit includes several
safety measures to avoid unintended robot movements:

- Teleoperation is disabled by default, to avoid accidental clicks. In
  order to start teleoperating a robot, you must press the button for
  one of the active Teleop modes to expressly enable active
  teleoperation.
- Teleoperation will be automatically disabled if the network round trip
  time to the robot is higher than 15 seconds. The currently measured
  round trip time value is displayed at the top of the teleoperation
  control.
- Even while performing teleoperation, if the message sent from the user
  interface is delayed for any reason and the robot receives it more
  than one second after it was sent, the command will be discarded on
  the robot side, preventing an unintended delayed movement.

In addition, teleoperation will be blocked unless the following
conditions are met:

- Odometry information is being published by the robot and InOrbit can
  decode it properly
- Robot movement as indicated by robot odometry matches the amount of
  distance intended by InOrbit’s teleoperation commands

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Experimental features</span>

If you are interested in trying out any additional experimental feature
or have any input or questions, please contact our support team at
[support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai).

 

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="button-container-id-time-capsule" class="hs-accordion__item"
aria-expanded="">

<span id="time-capsule" class="anchor"></span>

Time Capsule <span class="hs-accordion__item-icon"></span>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

Understanding the environment and behavior of a robot at a given point
in time is critical to solving some of the more difficult problems that
AMRs face today.  InOrbit Time Capsule makes this easier by efficiently
capturing, storing, and visualizing the critical information relevant to
a robot’s behavior:  

- Maps annotated with path, position, and incident detail
- Audit log details relevant to a selected robot and time range
- Timeline charts of system and network performance data
- Mission durations within the selected timeframe

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Creating your Time Capsule Dashboard</span>

InOrbit provides a preconfigured dashboard widget for use with Time
Capsule.  To enable it, follow these steps:

- Select Settings \> Robot Data \> Dashboards
- Select or create the dashboard to hold your Time Capsule
  - Remember to validate Dashboard visibility for the dashboard that
    will contain your Time Capsule widget, so people in the applicable
    roles have access
- Create a new Section within the dashboard for the Time Capsule widget
  - Time Capsule is available in the Section Scope dropdown menu.
  - After selecting the Time Capsule scope for your Section, no
    additional widgets may be added to this section
- Select the preconfigured layout for your Time Capsule section  
  <img
  src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=600&amp;name=39.png"
  style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
  srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=300&amp;name=39.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=600&amp;name=39.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=900&amp;name=39.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=1200&amp;name=39.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=1500&amp;name=39.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/39.png?width=1800&amp;name=39.png 1800w"
  sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="39" />
  - Basic:  Map, Audit Log, System Performance and Network Performance
    Widgets
  - Full:  All Basic layout widgets plus the Mode widget
  - Experimental: At InOrbit, one of our core values is to Always Be
    Learning.  The Experimental layout is one example of how we apply
    this value.  Currently the Experimental layout includes all Full
    layout widgets as well as a Key-value Sources widget.  In this
    layout, we will continue to experiment with different combinations
    of widgets to bring you the most value possible from your InOrbit
    Time Capsules.  

 

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Using the Time Capsule control widget</span>

The Time Capsule control widget contains several options you can choose
between to customize your visualization.  

- Robot picker:  This dropdown inherits the selected robot from your
  other dashboards while also providing the option to switch between
  robots without leaving your Time Capsule dashboard. 
- Date Range picker:  Select the start and end datetimes for your Time
  Capsule to cover.  
- Visualization picker:  This dropdown allows you to select the map
  overlay you want to see for the selected robot and datetime range.   
  <img
  src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=650&amp;name=40.png"
  style="width: 650px;" loading="lazy"
  srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=325&amp;name=40.png 325w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=650&amp;name=40.png 650w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=975&amp;name=40.png 975w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=1300&amp;name=40.png 1300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=1625&amp;name=40.png 1625w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/40.png?width=1950&amp;name=40.png 1950w"
  sizes="(max-width: 650px) 100vw, 650px" width="650" alt="40" />  
  The overlay options are:
  - ***Position*** shows the exact location of the selected robot at
    different points in time within your defined time range.  
  - ***Position (heatmap)*** highlights with colors the areas on the map
    where the robot spent the most time within your defined time range. 
    Warmer colors (yellow, orange, red) indicate more time while cooler
    colors (green, blue) indicate less time.  
  - ***Path*** shows the specific path(s) a robot took during the time
    range.  
  - ***Incidents*** highlights points on the map where incidents were
    logged by the robot during the time range.  
- Map picker - This dropdown allows you to select the map over which you
  want to see your data visualized.  The options available in this
  dropdown will be specific to the robot and datetime range you have
  selected.    
    

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Reviewing an incident with Time Capsule</span>

Opening a Time Capsule directly from your incident widget is as easy as
a single click.  To explore an incident in Time Capsule:

- Expand an incident found in your incident widget  
  <img
  src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=600&amp;name=41.png"
  style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
  srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=300&amp;name=41.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=600&amp;name=41.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=900&amp;name=41.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=1200&amp;name=41.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=1500&amp;name=41.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/41.png?width=1800&amp;name=41.png 1800w"
  sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="41" />
- Click on **Open Time Capsule**
- You’ll be taken to your Time Capsule dashboard with the appropriate
  robot selected and a time range starting 5 minutes before the incident
  and ending 5 minutes after the incident.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Exploring a Time Capsule</span>

Every Time Capsule is an opportunity to explore the environment in which
your robot was operating.  The data in each of the widgets shown is
correlated with other widgets in your Time Capsule dashboard.  As you
hover over an entry in the Robot Log or the Timeline widgets, you can
observe any correlating entries in the other widgets on the screen.  

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=650&amp;name=42.png"
style="width: 650px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=325&amp;name=42.png 325w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=650&amp;name=42.png 650w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=975&amp;name=42.png 975w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=1300&amp;name=42.png 1300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=1625&amp;name=42.png 1625w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/42.png?width=1950&amp;name=42.png 1950w"
sizes="(max-width: 650px) 100vw, 650px" width="650" alt="42" />

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Options for setting the datetime range</span>

When exploring different Time Capsule datetime ranges, you have several
options:

- You can set the start and end datetimes as specified above
- Selecting an entry in the Mode widget (found in Full and Experimental
  layouts) will automatically adjust the time range to the interval
  associated with that entry
- Selecting or dragging on a timeline widget will automatically adjust
  to the selected datetime range 

Clicking **Open Time Capsule** from the incident widget will set the
time range to 5 minutes before and 5 minutes after the incident.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="button-container-id-robops-copilot" class="hs-accordion__item"
aria-expanded="">

<span id="robops-copilot" class="anchor"></span>

RobOps Copilot <span class="hs-accordion__item-icon"></span>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit RobOps Copilot enhances your
interaction with InOrbit by providing a conversational interface to run
queries from aggregated robot data to expose details about your robot
fleet. </span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Accessible through the InOrbit Control web
interface and popular messaging platforms like Slack, this AI-powered
assistant leverages the latest large language models (LLMs), allowing
you to ask detailed questions in your preferred natural language using
everyday speech and receive detailed answers with the latest data
available about various aspects of your robot operations. Conversation
can refine analysis further, exploring robot missions, incidents, robot
health, audit logs, tags, and telemetry to drive key optimization
insights quickly.</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Find information here on integrating and
using InOrbit RobOps Copilot with InOrbit Control and through supported
messaging apps. </span>

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Getting Started</span>

##### <span style="font-size: 14px; color: #666666;">Requirements</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit Control web interface:  To use
InOrbit RobOps Copilot users must have an active InOrbit account with
the RobOps Copilot feature activated and have been granted user-based
access to RobOps Copilot by their organization.</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Messaging interface: While using InOrbit
RobOps Copilot in supported messaging apps is not required, it may be
useful for team-based data review and collaboration. InOrbit RobOps
Copilot currently supports integration with Slack. Additional messaging
applications may be supported in future updates of InOrbit RobOps
Copilot. </span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Slack:  To use InOrbit RobOps Copilot
users must have an active InOrbit account with the RobOps Copilot
feature activated, and have been granted user-based access to RobOps
Copilot by their organization. To integrate with Slack users must have
an active Slack account, and have added Copilot to their organization’s
shared workspace. </span>

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit is not affiliated with Slack
Technologies LLC, find more information about Slack at</span>
[https://slack.com/](https://slack.com/)

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Features</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit RobOps Copilot allows
comprehensive inquiries in natural language on topics such as:</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">**Missions**: Ask about current,
  pending, and completed missions.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Incidents**: Get details on anomalies
  or reported incidents.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Robots**: Inquire about specific
  robots, their status, and operational metrics.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Audit Logs**: Access audit logs to get
  insights into actions taken to solve incidents.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Tags**: Query and manage tags
  associated with different robots.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Telemetry**: Perform historical
  analysis on any robot property.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Time Capsule**: Request snapshots of
  Time Capsule to view in RobOps Copilot.</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Multi-user chat support</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">While talking to Copilot on messaging
apps, you can invite colleagues into the conversation. In this way, all
participants can contribute by asking or refining questions
collaboratively using the same session context. This collaborative
feature is a powerful way to share insights with authorized users on
your team.</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Seamless Conversation Transition from Messaging to  InOrbit Control</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Answers in a messaging interface may
contain references to specific missions, robots, incidents, or Time
Capsules that include links directing you to InOrbit Control. This may
allow authorized users to view more details on a topic while the
conversation continues with Copilot. The session conversation will
automatically transfer and open in the InOrbit RobOps Copilot web
interface.  </span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Results Sharing</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit RobOps Copilot often generates
insightful answers or reports in response to complex questions. Users on
the web interface can create a shareable link to reports generated
through conversation. Selecting the ‘share’ icon will create a link that
can be opened by anyone using the RobOps Copilot web interface.  This
allows critical data to be easily saved for future reference, or shared
with authorized colleagues.</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Query Bookmarks</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Once a query has been refined to produce a
report or generate the answers you need, you can save the query with a
bookmark.  Simply select the ‘bookmark’ icon and add a personalized name
to your bookmark for easy reference. This allows for a quick recall of
previously executed queries when needed again. </span>

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Integration with Slack</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Once requirements are met users can
interact with InOrbit RobOps Copilot via Slack:</span>

1.  <span style="color: #666666;">Begin by opening a chat window and
    typing a direct message to RobOps Copilot to start a conversation 
    By default it will be named @RobOps Copilot. Once open, you can
    invite other authorized users to the conversation.</span>
    - <span style="color: #666666;">Slack users who are not currently
      authorized to access InOrbit RobOps Copilot may still be invited
      to join a Slack conversation with Copilot as an observer only,
      however, they will not be permitted to pose any questions to
      Copilot</span>
2.  <span style="color: #666666;">Use natural language to query RobOps
    Copilot for instant results. Alternatively, use the</span> [Slack
    command
    interface](https://api.slack.com/automation/cli/quick-reference#:~:text=The%20command%20line%20interface%20allows,create%20triggers%2C%20and%20query%20datastores.)
    <span style="color: #666666;">by typing appropriately formatted
    commands directly in the chat box.  </span>  
    <span style="color: #666666;">Example commands:</span>
    - <span style="color: #666666;">\`/copilot status of robot
      ID\`</span>
    - <span style="color: #666666;">\`/copilot list all incidents today
      at location 285\`</span>

<img
src="https://lh7-us.googleusercontent.com/oFzCDw3HFW6Qoc1o4e9g1iG4m4xZEy8rak5EfTsgUgB-3Z5c1Ad7AzlrRAtBXK95xM0jPvAQmSpMkeweW9H7fZwfbAIKxqZFKMuIwxJOXhi_KDB9SQDiOGScvqG-KUCeLymT0v7h-vhYVu0IsbhKRPA"
style="width: 568px; height: auto; max-width: 100%;" loading="lazy"
width="568" height="426" />

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Integration with InOrbit Control</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Once requirements are met, InOrbit RobOps
Copilot can be accessed directly through the InOrbit Control web
interface:</span>

1.  <span style="color: #666666;">Getting started:</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">After logging into</span> [InOrbit
Control](https://control.inorbit.ai/)<span style="color: #666666;">,
click on the floating **Orbito** icon at the bottom right of the InOrbit
Control screen and a chat interface will appear. </span>

2.  <span style="color: #666666;">Interacting with InOrbitRobOps
    Copilot:</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Using natural language, type queries into
the chat window to explore your robot and fleet data.</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Your chat window may be closed or
minimized at any time so that you can continue using InOrbit Control
normally. If a RobOps Copilot chat window is closed and reopened during
the same session, your conversation will resume uninterrupted.</span>

<img
src="https://lh7-us.googleusercontent.com/jKf0dfYn0NZPyASHV-btGui6_t7mpu7nzNl1jT_UK9vogSlog59aInEbJmSviJ3Pn5QWhkv4dIjLedBRSe6g9vEf0Ka_Pjuwh3IGbbWrPgb5tkBCO-ElRC2r3XnPHbmn1cxwiVu6UMWTWBEcj3REe7I"
style="width: 596px; height: auto; max-width: 100%;" loading="lazy"
width="596" height="447" />

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Sample Queries</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Checking Mission Details:</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">Show details for mission ID 456.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">Show me the list of all missions from
  robots of model x, from the last 48h at warehouse 125.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">Tell me the average execution time for
  picking missions over the last week.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">How many missions did robot xyz run last
  week?</span>
  - <span style="color: #666666;">How many of those failed?</span>
  - <span style="color: #666666;">List the failed ones.</span>
  - <span style="color: #666666;">Tell me the average mission duration
    of these failed missions.</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Checking Robot Status:</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">What is the current status of robot
  123?</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">What was the average battery level of
  robot x when its missions finished during last week? </span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Audit Logs</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">Did robot xyz have any incidents last
  week?</span>
  - <span style="color: #666666;">What was the last incident it
    had?</span>
  - <span style="color: #666666;">List the actions taken to solve this
    incident.</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Incident Reports:</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">List all incidents from the last 24
  hours.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">Show me all the actions taken to solve
  incident X</span>

#### <span style="font-size: 16px; color: #666666;">Security and Privacy</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">RobOps Copilot is built with
industry-standard security practices to ensure your data is
protected:</span>

- <span style="color: #666666;">**Data Encryption**: All data
  transmitted between your devices, InOrbit Control, and supported
  messaging apps is encrypted with industry-standard cryptographic
  algorithms.</span>
- <span style="color: #666666;">**Role/User Based Access Controls**:
  Configure who can interact with InOrbit RobOps Copilot and what
  information they can access. Learn
  <a href="https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#look-at-inorbit"
  style="color: #666666;">more</a>.</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">Reach out to us to learn more abou</span>t
[InOrbit security
protocols](mailto:support@inorbit.ai)<span style="color: #666666;">.</span>

<span style="color: #666666;">InOrbit is committed to continuously
improving RobOps Copilot and values your input.</span> [Contact our
team](https://www.inorbit.ai/contact?hsLang=en)<span style="color: #666666;">
if you have questions or feedback.</span>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="button-container-id-robot-based-dashboard-customization"
class="hs-accordion__item" aria-expanded="">

<span id="robot-based-dashboard-customization" class="anchor"></span>

Robot-based dashboard customization
<span class="hs-accordion__item-icon"></span>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">When
managing a fleet with various types of robots—such as cleaning robots,
inventory scanners, material movers, forklifts, and robotic arms—each
type will <span style="font-size: 12px;">have</span> unique data that is
crucial to its operation. These robots may come from different
manufacturers or the same one, but the data requirements will vary by
robot type.</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">For
instance, “water level” and “detergent level” are critical for cleaning
robots but irrelevant for material movers. Similarly, monitoring battery
percentage is essential for Autonomous Mobile Robots (AMRs) but
unnecessary for stationary robotic arms..</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
concept of “Robot-based Customization” ensures that when a specific
robot is selected, the dashboard displays data unique and relevant to
that robot. Irrelevant data is dynamically omitted, adapting to the
selected robot. This customization maximizes
<span style="font-size: 12px;">the</span> dashboard’s value by tailoring
it to the specific needs of each robot. Depending on the robot type,
data within a widget may change, entire widgets may disappear, or entire
sections of the dashboard may appear or vanish based on
relevance.</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">"Robot-based
Customization” can be implemented through
<span style="font-size: 12px;">Configuration</span> as Code using Config
APIs. This involves applying conditional properties to widgets and
sections by specifying appropriate tags.</span>

<span style="font-size: 14px; font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><span style="color: #425b76;"><span style="color: #666666;">This
powerful feature is essential for managing complex robot operations
within a diverse fleet, where different types of robots perform various
missions. For more details on using Config APIs, refer to our technical
documentation in the</span> </span>[Developer
Portal](https://developer.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#robot-based-customization)<span style="color: #000000;">.</span></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 16px;">Customization
Examples</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 14px;">Fleet
dashboard</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
Fleet widget in the Fleet dashboard is essential for obtaining an
overview of your robot fleet’s health. By configuring statuses for
different robot types through the Config API, you can enable different
status sets to appear based on the selected robot. This allows you to
seamlessly monitor a diverse fleet with various robot types and data
sets through a single pane of glass.
<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;">Two screenshots
below show different types of robots show unique status sets when
selected.</span></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=1440&amp;height=920&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png"
style="height: auto; max-width: 100%; width: 1440px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=720&amp;height=460&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 720w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=1440&amp;height=920&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 1440w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=2160&amp;height=1380&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 2160w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=2880&amp;height=1840&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 2880w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=3600&amp;height=2300&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 3600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/small%20status%20set.png?width=4320&amp;height=2760&amp;name=small%20status%20set.png 4320w"
sizes="(max-width: 1440px) 100vw, 1440px" width="1440" height="920"
alt="small status set" /></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;"><span style="font-size: 11px;">The
screen above shows a selected robot, with a status set that includes:
mission, battery, RAM usage, localization,
</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">ping
</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">time,
and</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">
</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">ROS status.</span></span>

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=1440&amp;height=920&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png"
style="height: auto; max-width: 100%; width: 1440px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=720&amp;height=460&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 720w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=1440&amp;height=920&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 1440w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=2160&amp;height=1380&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 2160w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=2880&amp;height=1840&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 2880w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=3600&amp;height=2300&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 3600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/large%20status%20set.png?width=4320&amp;height=2760&amp;name=large%20status%20set.png 4320w"
sizes="(max-width: 1440px) 100vw, 1440px" width="1440" height="920"
alt="large status set" />

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;"><span style="font-size: 11px;">The
screen above shows a different selected robot, this time with a
</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">status</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">
set which</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">
</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">includes: robot is stopped,
localization, disk usage, RAM usage, fresh water,
battery</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">,</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">
docking, update failed, laser front, laser back.</span></span>

### <span style="color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 14px; font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;">Robot dashboard</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
Robot dashboard provides in-depth details of the selected robot. Using
the Config API, you can dynamically adjust the dashboard based on the
selected robot. The following elements can be adjusted:</span>

- <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">Different
  data within the same widget</span>
- <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">Different
  widgets within a section</span>
- <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">Different
  sections within the dashboard</span>
- <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">Widget
  width (based on a 12-column grid)</span>
- <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">Widget
  height</span>

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot%20db%20saturn1.png?width=1440&amp;height=920&amp;name=robot%20db%20saturn1.png"
style="height: auto; max-width: 100%; width: 1440px;" loading="lazy"

<span style="font-size: 11px; color: #666666; font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;">The
Robot dashboards above show different data within the same widget,
different widgets and different sections as a different robot is
selected in each image.</span>

### <span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 14px;">Navigation dashboard</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
Navigation dashboard offers various actions for a robot, such as
teleoperation and relocalization, along with detailed navigation-related
information like camera images and speedometers. The availability and
relevance of these controls and information depend on the robot type.
The Config API ensures that only relevant information is displayed,
reducing clutter in the navigation widget and allowing you to focus on
what’s important for the specific robot you are monitoring.</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=1440&amp;height=800&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png"
style="height: auto; max-width: 100%; width: 1440px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=720&amp;height=400&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 720w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=1440&amp;height=800&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 1440w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=2160&amp;height=1200&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 2160w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=2880&amp;height=1600&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 2880w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=3600&amp;height=2000&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 3600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png?width=4320&amp;height=2400&amp;name=nav%20db%20conditional%20-%20teleop.png 4320w"
sizes="(max-width: 1440px) 100vw, 1440px" width="1440" height="800"
alt="nav db conditional - teleop" /></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><img

style="height: auto; max-width: 100%; width: 1440px;" loading="lazy"

sizes="(max-width: 1440px) 100vw, 1440px" width="1440" height="800"
alt="nav db conditional - no teleop" /></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;"><span style="font-size: 11px;">The
first Navigation dashboard shows teleoperation controls, relocalization
options and a D-pad controller, while the 2nd dashboard shows none of
these features. This indicates that the selected robot in the second
dashboard either</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">
</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">does
not</span><span style="font-size: 12px;">
</span><span style="font-size: 11px;">have those capabilities, or does
not allow access to those features.</span></span>

### <span style="color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 14px; font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;">Time Capsule</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
Config API allows extensive customization of Time Capsule, determining
what data is shown and correlated. This can significantly enhance root
cause analysis capabilities, as the Time Capsule dashboard is tailored
to display the unique and specific data details of the selected robot
when reviewing historical data.</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><img

sizes="(max-width: 1440px) 100vw, 1440px" width="1440" height="1707"
alt="TC with 3 cameras" /></span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;"><img

srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=720&amp;height=987&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 720w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=1440&amp;height=1974&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 1440w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=2160&amp;height=2961&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 2160w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=2880&amp;height=3948&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 2880w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=3600&amp;height=4935&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 3600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/TC%20with%206%20cameras.png?width=4320&amp;height=5922&amp;name=TC%20with%206%20cameras.png 4320w"

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">The
first Time Capsule dashboard includes 3 cameras, a map and logs, while
the second Time Capsule has 6 small cameras followed by the State
Transition widget and Key-value sources. These unique layouts are
dynamically loaded on the fly when a different robot is selected for
review in Time Capsule.</span>

### <span style="color: #666666;">**<span style="font-size: 14px; font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif;">Settings</span>**</span>

<span style="font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif; color: #666666;">When
Config APIs are used to dynamically configure dashboards based on robot
selection, the Settings screens will appear different. Since Config
API-based changes can only be made through code using the InOrbit CLI
and not from the UI, these configurations are displayed as a code block
when the “Show code” option is clicked.</span>
