This option is included in the following widgets:

- Timeline
- Incident Timeline
- KPI indicators
- Audit Log
- Key/Value sources
- Map
- Vitals
- Modes and tags
- ROS Diagnostics
- Locations
- Fleet status
- Robot actions

Despite this button appearing in all the widgets mentioned above, it
only can be toggled in the Timeline, Incident Timeline and Audit Log
widgets.  

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Navigation dashboard</span>*

The **Navigation dashboard** lets you monitor a robot via **Map** and
**Camera** views, as well as other key data that is relevant during
navigation. It’s easy to switch between robots by using the robot
selector in the upper left.  At the right side of the bar, you’ll find
the **Hi-rez** toggle button (optional feature you can request to add)
to temporarily improve the quality of the primary camera , the map
selector, the map/camera button to switch between featuring the map or
the primary camera, the full-screen button for a deeper experience, and
the settings button to configure your navigation preferences.

In addition to executing one-click actions, you can relocalize the robot
if it gets lost, or use teleoperation to assist the robot with
navigation.

**<span style="color: #000000;"><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=600&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=300&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=600&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=900&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=1200&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=1500&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20A%20look%20at%20InOrbit%20Control/Navigation/nav_overview_stretch.png?width=1800&amp;name=nav_overview_stretch.png 1800w"

<span style="color: #000000;">Clicking on the

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Exit full-screen navigation</span>*

Clicking again on the full-screen view button takes you back to the
**Navigation dashboard**.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Navigation considerations for Free Edition</span>*

The Navigation Dashboard in Free Edition is a limited version relative
to the Standard Edition described above. It consists of a single map and
camera view, and does not include the  Hi-rez option or full-screen
navigation because it is intended only for monitoring the robot, without
any interaction.

With only these simple steps, InOrbit gives you near near instant
results for managing your robot fleet:

    account.</a>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

Follow these steps to get your robots InOrbit.

##### What you need

- Internet access
- The starting hyperlink from
  InOrbit, [https://control.inorbit.ai](https://control.inorbit.ai/)
- A Google account

##### Steps

1.  In your browser, go to [InOrbit
    Control](https://control.inorbit.ai/).
2.  Click **Sign in with Google**.
3.  In the displayed dialog, click the desired Google account name to
    use to sign in.
4.  On the **"Preparing for launch...**" page, enter the name of your
    company or keep the default.
5.  Click **Launch InOrbit**.

##### Results

One or more InOrbit Control Dashboards are ready for you to add robots
to your InOrbit fleet, configure settings, view metrics and KPIs, and
more.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

Here’s how you add a new robot to your InOrbit fleet. The process
involves copying and running a command on your robot's command line to
install the InOrbit agent.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">What you need</span>

- Internet access from your robot
- Shell access on your robot
- <span style="color: #807575;">The Owner or Administrator role in
  InOrbit Control</span>

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Steps</span>

1.  Log in to [InOrbit Control](https://control.inorbit.ai/).
2.  Select the Fleet dashboard to find and click <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=79&amp;name=new_robot_button.png"
    style="width: 79px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=40&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 40w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=79&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 79w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=119&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 119w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=158&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 158w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=198&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 198w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Add%20a%20robot%20to%20your%20fleet/new_robot_button.png?width=237&amp;name=new_robot_button.png 237w"
    sizes="(max-width: 79px) 100vw, 79px" width="79"
    alt="new_robot_button" />.
3.  Select and copy the displayed curl command.
4.  Log in to your robot's Linux shell.
5.  At the command line, paste the copied curl command.
6.  Hit enter to continue through the InOrbit agent installation
    process. See notes below.
7.  After the installation is complete, you are returned to the shell
    command line.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Notes</span>

- You are prompted to enter your username and other other information
  needed by the installer.
- You are kept informed of the installation progress, including
  installation of software required by the InOrbit robot agent.
- You might be instructed to enter various commands, such as
  <span style="font-family: 'Courier New', Courier, monospace; font-size: 12px;">sudo</span>,
  as required by the installer.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Results</span>

- The InOrbit agent is now installed on this robot.
- The robot's host name is displayed in the Fleet widget in InOrbit
  Control.
- InOrbit begins collecting data about your robot for you to examine on
  InOrbit Control.

Please
see <a href="/docs#advanced-agent-configuration" rel="noopener">Advanced
agent configuration</a> for further information on customizing the
agent.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

After you've added a robot, you can see its operational status and vital
signs directly on InOrbit Control.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">What you need</span>

- You need to have <a href="/docs#add-robot" style="color: #3b3fb1;"
  rel="noopener"><span>added a robot</span></a> to your fleet.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Steps</span>

1.  In your browser, go to InOrbit Control at
    <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>https://control.inorbit.ai</span></a>.
2.  If prompted, login with your Google account.
3.  Select the Robot Dashboard.
4.  Select the robot you want to check out using the Robot Control
    widget.
5.  Look for the Vitals widget, which is one of the default widgets in
    this dashboard.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Results</span>

InOrbit Control shows you at-a-glance details about your robot's
operational status:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=400&amp;name=robot_vitals.png"
style="width: 400px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=200&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=400&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=800&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 800w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=1000&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 1000w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20robot%20vital%20signs/robot_vitals.png?width=1200&amp;name=robot_vitals.png 1200w"
sizes="(max-width: 400px) 100vw, 400px" width="400"
alt="robot_vitals" />

Explore other valuable operational details about your robot, including
selected data on a timeline, current mode, incident list, and more.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

Here’s how you invite a new user to your InOrbit account. This provides
the authentication to enable colleagues to have individual access to a
shared InOrbit account.

##### What you need

- An active InOrbit account
- Owner privileges on the account
- Email addresses for any users you want to invite

##### Steps

##### <img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=600&amp;name=invite-users.png"
style="width: 600px; display: block; margin: 0px auto;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=300&amp;name=invite-users.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=600&amp;name=invite-users.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=900&amp;name=invite-users.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=1200&amp;name=invite-users.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=1500&amp;name=invite-users.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/invite-users.png?width=1800&amp;name=invite-users.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="invite-users" />

1.  Log in to [InOrbit Control](https://control.inorbit.ai/).
2.  Go to Settings-\>Admin-\>Company Details-\>People.
3.  Click the circled + sign.
4.  Enter 1 or more email addresses separated by commas or spaces.
5.  Select the role (e.g., Owner, Administrator, User) that the new
    user(s) will be granted.
6.  Click SEND INVITES.
7.  User(s) should receive an email to confirm and will be added after
    confirming.
8.  After confirming, users can log in to [InOrbit
    Control](https://control.inorbit.ai/) with the privileges for that
    role.

##### Notes

- Roles can be configurable, but Owner is the only default role
  authorized to invite users.
- Invited users will appear in a Pending state in the People list until
  they have confirmed.
- While a user is Pending, a login link is available on their entry that
  can be copied and directly shared with them as a backup.

##### Results

- The invited users have individual logins to the shared account.
- The invited users start with their assigned role, which can be
  modified by account Owners.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

The InOrbit agent is a small Python-based software that connects robots
to the cloud to enable reliable and low footprint bi-directional
communication with InOrbit cloud services.

# Debian Package Installation

We provide debian packages, currently supporting only **Ubuntu Xenial
with ROS Kinetic**, **Ubuntu Bionic with ROS Melodic**, **Ubuntu Focal
with ROS Noetic**, **Ubuntu Focal without ROS,**  **Ubuntu Jammy with
ROS 2 Humble** and **Ubuntu Jammy with ROS 2 Iron**.

These packages are intended for mature installations and deployments at
a larger scale. For smaller or development installations you may prefer
to follow the instructions at [Add a
Robot](https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#add-robot).

The main aspects of this installation are:

- It installs dependencies, runs in its own virtualenv and gets started
  automatically via systemd.
- Runs under a new user "inorbit" (default) or under a user id provided
  beforehand
- It installs the "binary files" (dist directory) under
  **`/usr/local/inorbit` **
- The configuration files go into **`/home/<userid>/.inorbit/local` **

Since in this installation method the binary files are installed with
root permissions, the agent remote updater doesn't work if this
installation method is used.

# Configuring the InOrbit Ubuntu repository 

The InOrbit agent is provided as the **inorbit-agent** Debian package,
available from the InOrbit repository. Please follow these instructions
to set-up the repository.

1.  <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 22.04 + ROS2
    Humble</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ jammy-ros2 main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

2.  Setup your sources.list

    Setup your computer to install software from the InOrbit repository:

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 16.04 + ROS
    Kinetic</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ xenial-main main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 18.04 + ROS
    Melodic</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ bionic-main main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 20.04 + ROS
    Noetic</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ focal-main main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 20.04 without
    ROS</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ focal-nonros main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 22.04 + ROS 2
    Humble</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ jammy-ros2 main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

    <span style="text-decoration: underline;">Ubuntu 22.04 + ROS 2
    Iron</span>

    `sudo sh -c 'echo "deb https://artifacts.inorbit.ai/debian/ jammy-iron-ros2 main" > /etc/apt/sources.list.d/inorbit-agent.list'` 

3.  Setup APT keys  
      
    `sudo apt-key adv --keyserver keyserver.ubuntu.com --recv-keys C45E9A6F71F2903944292BF7650F5571511D225F`   
      

4.  Install the following apt packages:  
    `sudo apt-get install -y apt-transport-https apt-utils`  
      

5.  Make sure your Debian package index is up-to-date:  
      
    `sudo apt update` 

# Installation

The expected flow is that the package is installed as root using
**apt-get**, and then in a separate step, a configuration file is added
to make the system work.

## <span style="font-size: 16px;">Installing InOrbit’s agent package</span>

Please make sure you configure your Ubuntu repositories first. Then you
can install InOrbit’s agent via apt-get with the following command:

`sudo apt install inorbit-agent` 

<span style="font-size: 16px;">Customized installation</span>

To install InOrbit’s agent with non-default installation options (e.g.:
customizing the userid to run under), we recommend using the following
command:

`sudo DEBIAN_FRONTEND=readline DEBIAN_PRIORITY=low apt-get install inorbit-agent` 

This will cause the Debian package manager to ask the user for
installation options interactively.

## <span style="font-size: 16px;">Customized unattended installation</span>

If unattended installation of the InOrbit agent with non-default
installation options are required, we recommend following the
instructions under **Unattended Package Installation** in the [debconf
documentation](http://manpages.ubuntu.com/manpages/xenial/man7/debconf.7.html).

In summary:

- Do a pre-configuration first on a development computer to create the
  configuration options file, i.e.:

  `sudo apt-get download inorbit-agent` 

  `cp /var/cache/debconf/config.dat /tmp/config.dat.before` 

  `sudo dpkg-preconfigure inorbit-agent-<version>.deb -p low # configure options` 

  `diff /var/cache/debconf/config.dat /tmp/config.dat.before > inorbit.config.dat` 

- Deliver that configuration file together with the Debian installer and
  use it as a fallback DB option during installation to use the
  pre-created configuration options:

  `sudo DEBCONF_DB_FALLBACK='File{/tmp/inorbit.config.dat}'` 

  `dpkg -i inorbit-agent-<version>.deb` 

  Once this setup is ready, you can proceed with the steps described on
  the next section (Configuring the agent). Note that in case you
  disable systemd, you will need to start the agent manually and **keep
  it alive within a loop**.

# Configuring the agent

The configuration file goes in
`/home/inorbit/.inorbit/local/agent.env.sh`  and has the following form,
with the appropriate value for each variable:

`export INORBIT_ROS="noetic"`  
`export INORBIT_ID="<ROBOT_ID>"`  
`export INORBIT_KEY="<API_KEY>"`  
`export INORBIT_URL="https://control.inorbit.ai/"`  
`export INORBIT_VARIANT="main"` 

*<span style="text-decoration: underline;">Note</span>*: For ROS 2
environments you will also need to set the `ROS_DOMAIN_ID` value above.

The user is responsible for provisioning a file like this to each robot
with the corresponding Robot ID and API Key. You can ask InOrbit’s
support team for the API Key or otherwise get it from InOrbit control.
For the latter, click the ‘[Add a
Robot](https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#add-robot)’ button on Fleet
Status: the API key is the final parameter on the URL displayed.

`curl https://control.inorbit.ai/liftoff/<API KEY> | sh`

Please see
<a href="/docs#advanced-agent-configuration" rel="noopener">Advanced
agent configuration</a> for further information on customizing the agent
by setting additional options in **agent.env.sh**.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

***Locations*** enable robots to be grouped based on their geographic
location (a city or address that can be displayed on a map). Locations
can have multiple areas that represent different parts of your site such
as different floors. 

**Locations** have tags associated with them, like any
other **collection**, but that tag has an underlying physical location.
Adding that tag to any robot will enable grouping by **location** and
easily associating robots with individual or common sites.
Alternatively, uploading a robot map to a location from
the **Locations** **Dashboard **will automatically associate that robot
with the corresponding location. 

 

###### *Configure locations for your account*

1.  Use the **Locations** **Dashboard** to create a new location. Give
    your location a unique name; this can be whatever you need it to be,
    from a city to something that makes sense within your system, such
    as *Store \#1234*. Add an address to enable displaying the location
    on a world map. 

2.  Now use the **Robots** tab in **Settings** \> **Organization** and
    assign robots to this location.  **Note**: InOrbit also supports
    automatically assigning robots to locations if the location can be
    distilled from a data source, which is recommended as a fleet scale.

    For example:

    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=499&amp;height=109&amp;name=11.png"
    style="width: 499px; height: auto; max-width: 100%;" loading="lazy"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=250&amp;height=55&amp;name=11.png 250w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=499&amp;height=109&amp;name=11.png 499w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=749&amp;height=164&amp;name=11.png 749w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=998&amp;height=218&amp;name=11.png 998w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=1248&amp;height=273&amp;name=11.png 1248w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/11.png?width=1497&amp;height=327&amp;name=11.png 1497w"
    sizes="(max-width: 499px) 100vw, 499px" width="499" height="109"
    alt="11" />

######  

###### *Adding areas and maps*

Each location will have a default area when you create them. You can add
more areas to your location by navigation to the location, then **Edit
Location** \> **Site \> Add Area**

Once you have added one or more areas to your location, you can add maps
for the areas. There are two ways to add maps: 

1.  **Add robot map**

    You can directly upload a map from your robot if your robot builds a
    map. To do this, select your location and area, then **Edit location
    \>** **Add robot map**

    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=600&amp;height=348&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png"
    style="width: 600px; height: auto; max-width: 100%;" loading="lazy"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=300&amp;height=174&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=600&amp;height=348&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=900&amp;height=522&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=1200&amp;height=696&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=1500&amp;height=870&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png?width=1800&amp;height=1044&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2021-59-43-png.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" height="348" />

2.  **Upload an image  **
    If you cannot upload a map directly from the robot, or if you have a
    map/blueprint of your site that you want to upload, you can do so
    from by selecting **Edit location \> Upload Map. ** Here, you can
    upload the map image. You will also need to enter the map origin and
    resolution. If your map came from a robot, this information will be
    included in the metadata. The map origin describes the point
    relative to which the robot pose is reported. If unknown, you can
    set the origin to 0,0. The resolution describes how many meters an
    image pixel represents, it must be known to interpret an image as a
    map. 

You can edit an existing map from **Edit location \> Edit Map.**

###### *Displaying multiple robots on a shared map*

Robots report their pose relative to their own maps, however, it is
often important to see many robots on a single map so that you can
program complex behavior like **traffic rules **for you locations. To
view multiple robots on a single map, select **Edit location \>**
**Align maps.** This tool will help you align a robot map to the default
map for your area so that multiple robots can be seen on the default map
correctly. Adjust the map position and rotation until the maps are
visually aligned and save the result.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=600&amp;height=348&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png"
style="width: 600px; height: auto; max-width: 100%;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=300&amp;height=174&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=600&amp;height=348&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=900&amp;height=522&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=1200&amp;height=696&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=1500&amp;height=870&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png?width=1800&amp;height=1044&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202025-05-29%2022-19-59-png.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" height="348" />  
 

 

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

You can augment InOrbit’s default robot data with your own custom
settings to view specific information/robot attributes you want to see
on <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/" style="color: #3b3fb1;">InOrbit
Control</a>.

As described in
<a href="https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#look-at-inorbit"
style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>A look at InOrbit Control</span></a>, when
you add a new robot, InOrbit Control displays default data/information
for a robot, such as CPU Usage, HDD Usage, and RAM. These are called
“Built-in” attributes.

You can configure InOrbit to capture other desired attributes of
particular interest to you, such as system or robot metrics, sensor
readings, software component details, and application-specific values.
You customize your robot data via the Settings menus in InOrbit Control.
A custom attribute applies to all robots in your entire fleet. Custom
attributes can then be used for real-time monitoring, to visualize
values over time directly in InOrbit Control, to track overall fleet
status or to trigger notifications on events or when something is not
within expected tolerances.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Background: Data Sources</span>

InOrbit supports the following data sources. These data sources are
obtained from the Robot Operating System (ROS) installed by the InOrbit
agent on each robot. (For more information about ROS itself, see the
<a href="http://wiki.ros.org/" style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>ROS
Wiki</span></a>.) InOrbit also supports additional non-ROS data sources.
For assistance in configuring non-ROS data sources, contact InOrbit via
email at <span style="color: #3b3fb1;">support@inorbit.ai</span>.

|  |  |
|----|----|
| <span style="color: #ffffff;">Type of Data</span> | <span style="color: #ffffff;">Description</span> |
| System metrics | CPU usage, RAM usage, specific HDD partitions, or network interfaces. |
| ROS Diagnostics  | If you use ROS and have nodes that publish values in the /diagnostics or /diagnostics_agg topics, InOrbit can be configured to work with this data. |
| Custom key/value pairs | If you use ROS and want to view a specific data value from your robot, you can publish a std_msgs/String message with the form key=value to the /inorbit/custom_data/0 topic on the agent; for example, apples=10. |

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;"> </span>

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">What You Need</span>

- The <a href="https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#add-robot"
  style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit agent installed on the
  robot</span></a> whose data you want to customize
- The Owner or Administrator role in InOrbit Control

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Steps for Custom Robot Data</span>

Viewing custom data involves two steps:

- defining a customer robot attribute
- displaying that attribute in InOrbit Control

We illustrate this on a specific robot attribute for viewing: usage of
the eth0 network interface on your robot.

###### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Part 1: Define the eth0 (example) custom robot attribute</span>

1.  As Owner or Administrator, login to
    <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>.
2.  From the <img
    src="https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/qfHNE0K3qYiySqPj_Yqz0_tuiDYtBtIl4tfegheU4cYGAlXru0orGHIW7kSN49IPgo3MAPJIxfG-gHKCi31tRR05MnZKibikWkx5Jxce0C0IYLZiuSvRjsD5L9rBwIbdftSM0G8h"
    width="8" height="20" /> menu in the upper right, select Settings.  
    Result: You are positioned on the Robot Data tab, which is divided
    into sections: Data Sources, Status, and Dashboards.
3.  To add the new data source, under the Data Sources heading, click
    the **<img
    src="https://lh4.googleusercontent.com/zfveFEy_0_9quJVq9x91ZcOo9ibKfUi3L3bN1PVQYTdLrahUg9-AhQpmQkzFAtEHMt89XWr2Te9keJedXWDquAhtp2ClML46mCbexqBiUNh25PKA5WEx8uExN8xpD23fShebF1zv"
    width="21" height="20" />** sign.  
    Result: A new empty slot for the custom attribute is displayed.
4.  Define the attribute you want to add in either of two ways:
    1.  Select the pulldown menu at the right of the new slot to view
        predefined system attributes.
    2.  In the empty slot, begin typing the name of the attribute you
        want to add. InOrbit displays matching data source names that it
        discovers from your robots such as the different network
        interfaces, HDD partitions, ROS Diagnostics tools or custom
        key/value pairs.
5.  As an example, we want to see the eth0 data source. From the
    pulldown menu, find and select eth0. The custom attribute is added
    to the list of Data Sources  
      
    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=700&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png"
    style="width: 700px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=350&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=700&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=1050&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=1400&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=1750&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth0_addDatasource-1.png?width=2100&amp;name=eth0_addDatasource-1.png 2100w"
    sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700"
    alt="eth0_addDatasource-1" />  
      
6.  To add a more user-friendly name for the attribute, scroll to New
    Data source entry in the Data Sources list and enter the
    user-friendly name in the first field, e.g., “Network interface -
    eth0”. The user-friendly name is updated for any configured
    visualization widget.  
    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=700&amp;name=02.png"
    style="width: 700px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=350&amp;name=02.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=700&amp;name=02.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=1050&amp;name=02.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=1400&amp;name=02.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=1750&amp;name=02.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/02.png?width=2100&amp;name=02.png 2100w"
    sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="02" />  
      
    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=700&amp;name=03.png"
    style="width: 700px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=350&amp;name=03.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=700&amp;name=03.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=1050&amp;name=03.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=1400&amp;name=03.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=1750&amp;name=03.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/03.png?width=2100&amp;name=03.png 2100w"
    sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="03" />  
      
    Result: You have defined the new custom eth0 attribute, renamed
    “Network interface - eth0”, and InOrbit automatically saves the new
    configuration.

###### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Part 2: View the eth0 custom robot attribute on InOrbit Control and change its position</span>

###### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Add a new robot status to Fleet Status widget</span>

You can add a new data source to the Fleet Status widget based on the
new custom attribute defined in Data Sources. For this, go to Settings
\> Robot Data \> Status and click on the **<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=30&amp;name=04.png"
style="width: 30px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=15&amp;name=04.png 15w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=30&amp;name=04.png 30w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=45&amp;name=04.png 45w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=60&amp;name=04.png 60w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=75&amp;name=04.png 75w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/04.png?width=90&amp;name=04.png 90w"
sizes="(max-width: 30px) 100vw, 30px" width="30" alt="04" />**icon for
the new data source, “Network interface - eth0” in our example.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=800&amp;name=eth-Status.png"
style="width: 800px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=400&amp;name=eth-Status.png 400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=800&amp;name=eth-Status.png 800w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=1200&amp;name=eth-Status.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=1600&amp;name=eth-Status.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=2000&amp;name=eth-Status.png 2000w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/eth-Status.png?width=2400&amp;name=eth-Status.png 2400w"
sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" width="800" alt="eth-Status" />

 

To see the new status, go to InOrbit Control and select the ‘Fleet’
dashboard.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=800&amp;name=fleet_wide.png"
style="width: 800px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=400&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=800&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 800w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=1200&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=1600&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=2000&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 2000w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20View%20custom%20robot%20data%20-%20basics/fleet_wide.png?width=2400&amp;name=fleet_wide.png 2400w"
sizes="(max-width: 800px) 100vw, 800px" width="800" alt="fleet_wide" />

###### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #000000;">Add a new data source to Vitals or Timeline widgets</span>

To configure the visualization of the new attribute in a Vitals or
Timeline widget:

1.  Go to Settings \> Robot Data \> Dashboards.
2.  Select the Dashboard you’d like to add this attribute to.
3.  Expand the Section where you’d like to add this attribute. You can
    also create a new section if needed.
4.  To add the data to an existing widget, expand the Vitals or Timeline
    configuration and add the new attribute. Note that Vitals widget can
    only have four data sources. When adding a Timeline widget, the min
    number of lines must be set to at least 1.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=700&amp;name=05.png"
style="width: 700px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=350&amp;name=05.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=700&amp;name=05.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=1050&amp;name=05.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=1400&amp;name=05.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=1750&amp;name=05.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/05.png?width=2100&amp;name=05.png 2100w"
sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="05" />

 

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=700&amp;name=06.png"
style="width: 700px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=350&amp;name=06.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=700&amp;name=06.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=1050&amp;name=06.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=1400&amp;name=06.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=1750&amp;name=06.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/06.png?width=2100&amp;name=06.png 2100w"
sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="06" />

To change the position of the attribute within a widget or Timeline on
InOrbit Control:

1.  Go to Settings \> Robot Data \> Dashboards
2.  Select the tab for the Dashboard you’d like to update. Find the
    attribute in the Widget within the corresponding Section..
3.  Grab the = handle to the left of the attribute.
4.  Drag the attribute up or down in the Visualization list to the
    desired display position.

Result: Return to InOrbit Control to see the attribute in the new
position.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<div class="expand-content-height">

<div class="section howto">

InOrbit helps you keep track of the overall state of your robot fleet,
notifies you through different channels based on your preferences, and
makes it easier to intervene when necessary.

As discussed in
<a href="https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#view-custom-robot"
style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>View custom robot data</span></a>, in
addition to the default robot data sources, you might have created other
sources of data from your robots that you want to see on
<a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>.

You can set *thresholds* on the data sources to trigger warning or error
events when a threshold is exceeded and *incidents* of different
severity based on  those events. *Alerts* through different notification
channels can be triggered in case of an incident and *actions* to take
in order to resolve a problem can be automatically or manually executed.

- **Threshold** values can be set for each robot data source, such as
  disk space usage or camera state. Each threshold is defined by a
  *function*, which also depends on the data source. Some functions are
  as follows:
  - **max-time** is the length of time a condition must last before the
    threshold is exceeded.
  - **always** means that the condition is binary: either always on or
    always off.
  - **not equal** to a value you specify
- **Alert channels** are any of the following:
  - ***In App*** notification, i.e. within InOrbit Control itself.
  - ***Slack*** or ***Google Chat***, optional
  - Premium channels including
    <a href="#incident-mgmt-opsgenie" rel="noopener">Opsgenie</a> and
    <a href="#incident-mgmt-webhook-apis" rel="noopener">custom
    integrations</a> via API and webhooks
- **Actions** can be easily configured and invoked from within a
  notification or accessed directly from InOrbit Control widgets. See
  <a href="#configure-actions" rel="noopener">Configure actions</a> for
  more details. 

**Example.** For a robot’s CPU usage, you might want to set these
values:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
| **<span style="color: #ffffff;">Event</span>** | **<span style="color: #ffffff;">Threshold</span>** | **<span style="color: #ffffff;">Alert channel</span>** | **<span style="color: #ffffff;">Action</span>** |
| Warning | 85% CPU usage for 60 seconds or more | In app | Run script on robot |
| Error | 95% CPU usage for 30 seconds or more | Slack | Restart InOrbit agent |

 

##### <span style="font-size: 13.5pt; color: #000000;">Setting up thresholds and incidents</span>

These are the steps to define thresholds, incidents, alert channels and
actions.

##### <span style="font-size: 13.5pt; color: #000000;">What you need</span>

- Data sources for which you want to define incident management.
- On-robot scripts you want to run (if any).
- Slack channel(s) to receive alerts if using Slack integration. If you
  want to send alerts to it, you must have Slack installed and running.

##### <span style="font-size: 13.5pt; color: #000000;">Steps</span>

Automated incident responses have two general parts:

- On the Settings \> Robot Data tab
  - Warning and error thresholds for each data source
- On the Settings \> Insights tab
  - Incidents definitions

##### <span style="font-size: 13.5pt; color: #000000;">Define thresholds</span>

1.  As administrator, login to <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>.
2.  In the upper right, click Settings in the 3 vertical dots menu
3.  Click the Robot Data tab.
4.  Select the Status section on the left menu.
5.  Click the (+) icon to add a new status
6.  Select a *data source*.
7.  From the function dropdown menu, select the desired function. The
    function depends on the data source. For example, CPU and disk usage
    use the max-time function.
8.  For the *function* field, select the function desired for this alert
9.  For the *error at* field, enter the error threshold.  
    For example, for CPU, enter a percentage.
10. For the *warning at* field, enter the warning threshold.  
    For example, for CPU usage, enter a percentage.
11. For the *for sec.* field, enter the length of time that the warning
    or error condition must last before the threshold is exceeded.
12. For *status label*, keep the default label or enter a custom label
    for this data source.

##### <span style="font-size: 13.5pt; color: #000000;">Define incidents</span>

1.  As administrator, login to <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>.
2.  In the upper right, click Settings.
3.  Click the Insights tab.
4.  Select the Incidents section on the left menu.
5.  Click on the (+) icon to create a new incident definition or expand
    an existing incident.
6.  Enter incident name and select an existing status as described in
    the Define threshold step above.
7.  For the ERROR block:
    - In App
    - Slack
    - Google Chat
    - Premium channels

    <!-- -->

    - Select a severity level between SEV0 (most serious) and SEV3 
      (least serious)
    - Select one or more alert channels
    - In the *When triggered, run* field, select actions that you want
      to trigger automatically
    - In the *When notified, user can run* field, select actions that
      you want to recommend to the end user. Note that access to
      recommended actions depends on the alert notification channel
      selected.
8.  For the WARNING block, repeat the preceding step.

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

When you create your account (see [Quick
start](https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#quick-start)), InOrbit will
start with a default configuration, which tracks only a few attributes
from your robots, such as CPU Usage, HDD Usage and Network Rate.

You can configure InOrbit to capture more relevant attributes for your
particular operation, such as additional system or robot metrics, sensor
readings, software component vitals or application-specific values.

Any attributes you configure can then be used for real-time monitoring,
to visualize their values over time, to track overall fleet status and
to trigger alerts based on events or when something is not within
expected values.

These configurations are managed in the Data Sources section of the
settings screen, accessible through Settings:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=700&amp;name=07.png"
style="width: 700px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=350&amp;name=07.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=700&amp;name=07.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=1050&amp;name=07.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=1400&amp;name=07.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=1750&amp;name=07.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/07.png?width=2100&amp;name=07.png 2100w"
sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="07" />

InOrbit currently supports the following data sources:

1.  System metrics  
    You can monitor CPU usage, RAM usage, specific HDD partitions and
    network interfaces.
2.  ROS Diagnostics values  
    If you use ROS and have nodes that publish values in the
    /diagnostics or /diagnostics_agg topics, InOrbit can be configured
    to pick up any of these values.
3.  Custom key/value pairs  
    If you are using ROS and want to publish an arbitrary value from
    your robot, you can publish a std_msgs/String message with the form
    key=value (e.g; apples=10) to the /inorbit/custom_data/0 topic on
    the agent.
4.  Other types / non-ROS  
    InOrbit also supports additional and non-ROS data sources, which can
    be enabled per customer request. Please let us know about your needs
    by writing to us at
    <span style="color: #3b3fb1;">support@inorbit.ai</span>.

In order to make it easier to configure, InOrbit lets you search over
the data sources that it can discover from your robots. Simply start
typing in the box to filter between the different network interfaces,
HDD partitions, ROS Diagnostics tools or custom key/value pairs:

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=700&amp;name=08.png"
style="width: 700px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=350&amp;name=08.png 350w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=700&amp;name=08.png 700w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=1050&amp;name=08.png 1050w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=1400&amp;name=08.png 1400w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=1750&amp;name=08.png 1750w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/08.png?width=2100&amp;name=08.png 2100w"
sizes="(max-width: 700px) 100vw, 700px" width="700" alt="08" />

NOTE: In order for InOrbit to be able to discover the available data
sources on your robot, it is important that the agent be running before
you attempt data source configuration.

 

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

InOrbit allows configuring custom **Actions** you can use to send
commands to any robot. These actions can be used to quickly and
efficiently resolve issues or incidents that occur as part of operating
your robot fleet, as well as allowing users to *nudge* a robot towards
the desired behavior.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px; color: #666666;">Overview</span>

InOrbit currently supports five distinct methods of triggering specific
robot behaviors and other interactions. These methods can easily be
configured with minimal technical knowledge, quickly enabling users to
send commands with the click of a button. See also the Robot Lock
premium feature. 

These actions can be made available for execution in the ROBOTS section
of InOrbit Control through the Robot Actions widget,

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_actions.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=300&amp;name=robot_actions.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_actions.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=900&amp;name=robot_actions.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=1200&amp;name=robot_actions.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=1500&amp;name=robot_actions.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions.png?width=1800&amp;name=robot_actions.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="robot_actions" />

The widget allows you to display actions organized in groups, that you
can define in **Settings \> Insights**. Each row shows the group name,
the number of actions associated with this group and also a button to
expand the section. When this button is clicked, all the actions in the
group are shown.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/robot_actions_expanded.png?width=600&amp;name=robot_actions_expanded.png"
style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"

Integration with other incident management systems such as Opsgenie and
custom systems is also available as a premium offering.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Setting up Actions</span>

There are many ways to define an action on InOrbit:

- **Publish** on a ROS topic  
  Publish a configurable string value through
  the/inorbit/custom_commands topic on the robot.
- **Run** script on agent  
  Run a shell script on the robot. Scripts must be placed in the
  \${HOME}/.inorbit/local/user_scripts directory.
- **Jump** to InOrbit page  
  Bring the operator to a specified page in InOrbit Control, such as the
  localization and navigation screen for a robot. Useful to allow you to
  dive deeper into a problem and perform the most effective resolution
  action.
- **Open** URL  
  Take the operator to the configured URL on a Web browser window.
  Useful to direct your operator to an internal system to continue the
  resolution protocol for a given incident.
- **Execute** a ROS service *(coming soon)  *
  Execute a ROS service on the robot.

To configure actions:

1.  Go to Settings \> Insights \> Actions
2.  Click the (+) icon to add a new action or select an existing action
3.  For the **name** field, enter the name for the new action, e.g.,
    **NEW ACTION**  
      
    **<img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=600&amp;name=create_action.png"
    style="width: 600px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=300&amp;name=create_action.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=600&amp;name=create_action.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=900&amp;name=create_action.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=1200&amp;name=create_action.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=1500&amp;name=create_action.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/create_action.png?width=1800&amp;name=create_action.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
    alt="create_action" />**
4.  For the **type** field,  select the desired action, e.g., **Run
    script on agent**  
      
    **<img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=600&amp;name=action_type.png"
    style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=300&amp;name=action_type.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=600&amp;name=action_type.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=900&amp;name=action_type.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=1200&amp;name=action_type.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=1500&amp;name=action_type.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_type.png?width=1800&amp;name=action_type.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="action_type" />**
5.  Each action is listed in a **section**, which is a grouping of
    similar actions
6.  Navigate to the newly created **action** and select an existing
    **section** or create a new **section**  
      
    **<img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=600&amp;name=action_edition.png"
    style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=300&amp;name=action_edition.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=600&amp;name=action_edition.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=900&amp;name=action_edition.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=1200&amp;name=action_edition.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=1500&amp;name=action_edition.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_edition.png?width=1800&amp;name=action_edition.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
    alt="action_edition" />**  
      
7.   Select the **Confirm execution** checkbox if you want the action to
    require user confirmation before executing the command. If the
    premium Robot Lock feature is enabled, you may optionally select
    **Requires Lock** to ensure there are no conflicting commands from
    multiple users.   
      
    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=600&amp;name=check_option.png"
    style="width: 600px;" loading="lazy"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=300&amp;name=check_option.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=600&amp;name=check_option.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=900&amp;name=check_option.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=1200&amp;name=check_option.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=1500&amp;name=check_option.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/check_option.png?width=1800&amp;name=check_option.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
    alt="check_option" />  
      
8.  Use the **embedded** dropdown to select which widgets should include
    this **action** - note that the selection of the **action** will
    cause the **section** to be included in the **action** dropdown in
    the selected widget  
    <img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=600&amp;name=09.png"
    style="width: 600px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=300&amp;name=09.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=600&amp;name=09.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=900&amp;name=09.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=1200&amp;name=09.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=1500&amp;name=09.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/09.png?width=1800&amp;name=09.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="09" />
9.  Select whether the **action** is **Always available**, if it should
    be available **Only in collections** that are specified by you, or
    if it should be available only when it's **Not in collections** that
    you select<img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=600&amp;name=10.png"
    style="width: 600px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=300&amp;name=10.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=600&amp;name=10.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=900&amp;name=10.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=1200&amp;name=10.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=1500&amp;name=10.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/10.png?width=1800&amp;name=10.png 1800w"
    sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="10" />
10. Each **action** can be configured with a specific **parameter** that
    can be changed or set as needed, including parameters that can be
    provided by the end user when executing the action<img
    src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=600&amp;name=action_parameter.png"
    style="width: 600px;"
    srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=300&amp;name=action_parameter.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=600&amp;name=action_parameter.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=900&amp;name=action_parameter.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=1200&amp;name=action_parameter.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=1500&amp;name=action_parameter.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Configure%20actions/action_parameter.png?width=1800&amp;name=action_parameter.png 1800w"
