<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

Any additional options or environment variables required by scripts
meant to be executed by the InOrbit agent should be included in the
**agent.env.sh** file in order to become available to the InOrbit agent
executable. 

The default location of this file is
`${HOME}/.inorbit/local/agent.env.sh` . This will be different in case
you have customized the userid to run under or if you have set
`INORBIT_HOME` .

The InOrbit agent supports the following environment variables:

### ROS_CUSTOM_PATH

> #### Introduced in 1.11.0

Allows specifying a custom location for ROS when it is installed, for
example:

`export ROS_CUSTOM_PATH=/opt/mybot` 

### HTTP_PROXY

> #### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Introduced in 3.0.0</span>

This variable allows configuring the InOrbit agent to connect through a
non-transparent HTTP proxy such as NTLM.

When this variable is set, the agent will configure the MQTT client to
use websockets transport through a proxy server. This environment
variable needs to be defined with the IP address of the proxy server,
for example:

`export HTTP_PROXY="http://localhost:445"`

NOTE: the agent assumes that `HTTP_PROXY`  is using SSL.

### INORBIT_HOME

> #### Introduced in 3.5.0

Allows specifying a custom location to use as a data directory to store
agent logs and other configuration and runtime data. For example:

`export INORBIT_HOME="/mount/data/inorbit"` 

By default, the agent uses the `${HOME}/.inorbit`  directory within the
home directory of the user under which it runs.

### INORBIT_ENABLE_WATCHDOG

> #### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Introduced in 3.8.0</span>

Allows enabling an agent watchdog script, which checks INORBIT's agent
status and kills

it if it finds its MQTT connection socket stuck in  CLOSE_WAIT state for
more than 60 seconds. To enable the watchdog, the value of this
parameter must be set to “yes”, for example:

`export INORBIT_ENABLE_WATCHDOG="yes"` 

The watchdog will be disabled by default when this parameter is not set
or if it is explicitly set to "no".

### INORBIT_LOG_LEVEL

> #### Introduced in 3.8.0

Allows setting the logging level of inorbit_agent.log to any of the
[levels supported by Python’s logging
module](https://docs.python.org/3/library/logging.html#levels):

- “critical”
- “error”
- “warning”
- “info”
- “debug”

The logging level is “info” by default:

`export INORBIT_LOG_LEVEL="info"` 

### INORBIT_ACTIONS_PATH

> #### Introduced in 3.20.0

Allows configuring the directory where the agent will look for the
action scripts. If the agent detects it and confirms that the path is
valid\*, it will use that directory instead of the default
`~/.inorbit/local/user_scripts/. ` .

An example is:

`export INORBIT_ACTIONS_PATH="/home/myrobot/inorbit_actions"` 

\*Please make sure that the path exists and that the user under which
InOrbit’s agent is installed has execution permission for the action
scripts.

### INORBIT_ROS_UNREGISTER_PUBLISHERS

> #### Introduced in 3.21.0

As of agent version 3.21.0, the InOrbit agent will avoid unregistering
publishers when agentlets unload in order to work around a
[long-standing rospy known
issue](https://github.com/ros/ros_comm/issues/111).

This variable, when set to `TRUE` , allows falling back to the previous
behavior where each time an agentlet that has a publisher unloads, the
publisher will be unregistered from the ROS system.

**NOTE**: This may cause repetitive warning messages in /rosout and
inorbit.log

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<div class="expand-content-height">

<div class="section howto">

InOrbit allows individual robots to be locked while someone is sending
specific actions to it to prevent multiple people from executing
potentially conflicting actions.

**This feature is only available for Enterprise accounts**

##### Configuring Robot Lock

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=880&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png"
loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=440&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 440w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=880&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 880w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=1320&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 1320w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=1760&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 1760w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=2200&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 2200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/robot_lock_default-png.png?width=2640&amp;name=robot_lock_default-png.png 2640w"
sizes="(max-width: 880px) 100vw, 880px" width="880" />

Locks can be configured under Settings \> Insights \> Actions to be:

- **Disabled** - locks do not apply *(default)*
- **Selectable** - locked explicitly through the `LOCK` button
- **Automatic** - implicitly locked when a person sends an action that
  requires lock

Any action can be configured to require a lock on the robot.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=244&amp;name=lock-configure.png"
style="width: 244px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=122&amp;name=lock-configure.png 122w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=244&amp;name=lock-configure.png 244w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=366&amp;name=lock-configure.png 366w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=488&amp;name=lock-configure.png 488w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=610&amp;name=lock-configure.png 610w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/lock-configure.png?width=732&amp;name=lock-configure.png 732w"
sizes="(max-width: 244px) 100vw, 244px" width="244" /><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hubfs/robot_lock_user_defined_action-png.png"
loading="lazy" />

Check the `Requires Lock` box to specify which actions will implicitly
create a lock when used (Automatic mode) or require the robot to be
locked before running (Selectable mode).

##### Using Robot Lock

InOrbit currently supports the following capabilities around robot
locks:

##### Locking a robot

##### <img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=600&amp;name=lock_robot.png"
style="width: 600px; margin-top: 0px; margin-bottom: 0px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=300&amp;name=lock_robot.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=600&amp;name=lock_robot.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=900&amp;name=lock_robot.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=1200&amp;name=lock_robot.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=1500&amp;name=lock_robot.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/lock_robot.png?width=1800&amp;name=lock_robot.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="lock_robot" />

Any robot can be locked by clicking on the `LOCK` button, which toggles
to `UNLOCK` to easily remove the lock.

Other people can hover over the `UNLOCK` button to see who currently has
the lock on a robot and how long it has been in effect.

##### Unlocking a robot

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=600&amp;name=unlock_robot.png"
style="width: 600px; margin-top: 0px; margin-bottom: 0px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=300&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=600&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=900&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=1200&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=1500&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Robot%20Lock/unlock_robot.png?width=1800&amp;name=unlock_robot.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600"
alt="unlock_robot" /><img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=287&amp;name=unlock-button.png"
style="width: 287px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=144&amp;name=unlock-button.png 144w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=287&amp;name=unlock-button.png 287w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=431&amp;name=unlock-button.png 431w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=574&amp;name=unlock-button.png 574w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=718&amp;name=unlock-button.png 718w, https://www.inorbit.ai/assets/img/howto/unlock-button.png?width=861&amp;name=unlock-button.png 861w"
sizes="(max-width: 287px) 100vw, 287px" width="287" />

The person who locked the robot can unlock it by pressing
the `UNLOCK` button. This allows other people to claim the lock on the
robot.

###### Breaking the lock

If the robot is locked, an authorized person other than the person who
locked the robot can force the robot to be unlocked by pressing
the `UNLOCK` button.

In this case, there is a confirmation on unlocking a robot to ensure
that this is a deliberate action.

###### Lock expiring after a timeout

To prevent situations where a person who locked a robot forgets to
unlock it, locks are automatically released after a period of time (10
minutes) has passed since the last action on the robot.

 

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Introduction</span>

The results of mission execution are the main determinant of autonomous
robots’ usefulness. Data around mission performance also provides a
feedback mechanism to pinpoint issues that affect individual robots or
fleets and target opportunities to improve performance and reliability.

Missions vary considerably across applications, and could involve
picking a specific item, scanning an aisle for inventory, or cleaning an
area, as examples. Missions represent the work the robot does, and thus
what your company or your customers are counting on when investing in
robots.

As a platform, InOrbit offers different configurations to support
different customer scenarios, processing mission data from wherever it
is available, whether that is on the robots themselves or from the cloud
or an edge server.

InOrbit consolidates information from monitoring and controlling
missions to help bolster your understanding of your robot’s activity and
overall performance (observability/operation) and identifies areas for
improvement (optimization).

Missions can be started, cancelled, or otherwise managed via dedicated
actions or through the API as in the examples below. The focus of this
document is on *mission tracking* and its ability to provide visibility
into the performance of selected sets of missions as well as the
progress and data throughout any given mission

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Product Offering</span>

Mission Tracking is part of Enterprise Edition and can also be enabled
separately as an Add-On. Standard Edition includes the same Mission
Tracking capabilities with a lightweight version of Missions reporting.

When missions are enabled in InOrbit, mission information is available
throughout the platform. Focusing on the default dashboards in InOrbit
Control, missions are visible in several places:

- The **Fleet** and **Robot** dashboards have a **Missions** **widget**
  available with listings of current missions and filtering options to
  explore past missions.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=288&amp;name=missions_widget.png"
style="width: 288px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=144&amp;name=missions_widget.png 144w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=288&amp;name=missions_widget.png 288w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=432&amp;name=missions_widget.png 432w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=576&amp;name=missions_widget.png 576w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=720&amp;name=missions_widget.png 720w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/missions_widget.png?width=864&amp;name=missions_widget.png 864w"
sizes="(max-width: 288px) 100vw, 288px" width="288"
alt="missions_widget" />

- **Time Capsule** allows replaying a mission with all of its key data
  from that time interval.
- The **Missions** dashboard consists of several dedicated widgets
  - **Mission control** - Group, sort, filter missions to select the set
    to explore
  - **Mission KPIs** - Configurable KPIs for the primary metrics that
    reflect mission performance
  - **Mission details** - A table showing all selected missions with key
    data fields, including mission-specific data and the ability to
    launch Time Capsule

<!-- -->

-  

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png"
style="width: 640px; margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; display: block;"
loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=320&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 320w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 640w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=960&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 960w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=1280&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 1280w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=1600&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_dashboard.png?width=1920&amp;name=mission_dashboard.png 1920w"
sizes="(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px" width="640"
alt="mission_dashboard" />

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Basic data required to represent a mission</span>

Customers have different ways and terminology for  describing and
representing missions. They could be quite broad (e.g., perform a
multi-hour navigation of a region) or more akin to straightforward tasks
(e.g., pick an object). InOrbit has created a flexible model to
accommodate a wide range of scenarios, where Missions can fit whatever
scale your “mission” is.<span style="font-size: 13px;"></span>

InOrbit provides a generalized data model, with mostly optional fields
to map to your structures and terminology. There are some critical
fields for accurate tracking, and others that can enhance the monitoring
feedback, and in some cases it can be derived if your system does not
explicitly report it.<span style="font-size: 13px;"></span>

For example, missions have ***scheduled start times*** (whether they are
performed ad hoc or through a scheduler) and ***actual start and end
times*** to determine whether a robot was on that mission at a
particular time. Even the ***Mission*** ***name*** is optional, but it
is easy and recommended to specify a name, which generally makes it
easier to do correlations when monitoring multiple missions in real-time
or reviewing past missions.<span style="font-size: 13px;"></span>

Missions also have a single current ***state*** at any given time. This
is a freeform (customizable) label, although we offer a pre-defined set
of states, such as *Scheduled*, *In-Progress*, *Late*, and *Abandoned*.
Missions also have a current ***status***, which is normally derived
from the state, but could also be specified from your system. This is
either *OK*, *Warning*, or *Error*. This is similar to overall data
status in the InOrbit Platform, enabling consistent presentation and
mission-based tracking, alerts, and potentially manual or automated
actions for interventions.<span style="font-size: 13px;"></span>

Generally all labels and mappings are configurable, and we are happy to
help you set up whatever unique structure you require. The platform also
includes optional metadata for your specific scenario (e.g., number of
picked items, payload, completed scans, signal strength).

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Activating your data to be processed as missions</span>

Robot missions can have widely differing scenarios, particularly in how
they are triggered and where information is stored. InOrbit can track
data directly from robots or integrate with your infrastructure,
depending on where the information is made available. In some cases the
robot itself determines that it is starting a mission based on some
input (e.g., a front panel activated by a local operator). In other
cases, there can be a cloud component (e.g., a scheduler or  controller)
coordinating missions and ordering robots to start them.  It is possible
that the robot does not know about the scope of the mission, or there
isn’t a centralized component that knows about the missions.InOrbit
provides a flexible API to accommodate these scenarios. The main entry
point for missions is a REST [Mission Tracking
API](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#tag/missions), but in many
cases it is not necessary to invoke this API directly; this is explained
below

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Mission data on robots</span>*

If mission data is best reported from the robots, the metadata can be
published through the InOrbit agent. This involves publishing data
sources (like any other data in InOrbit) and configuring them to tie to
Mission data. For example, in a ROS environment, it is common to publish
some “robot state” as “starting mission” or “going back to dock”; along
with other metadata such as “delivery door number” or “route ID to
execute”. By configuring these data sources in InOrbit cloud, the API
calls are implicit (e.g., you can configure that publishing “robot
status=starting mission” implies a call to the start of a mission).

As an advanced scenario that some customers prefer, you can also
configure a pre-authenticated channel from the robot to the Missions API
by publishing JSON-encoded data which will be mapped directly to API
calls. This comes down to how you prefer to manage data on your robots
for external communication.

These two scenarios are configured through [Mission
Tracking](https://developer.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#configuring-mission-tracking)
Config API, and correspond to “derived” and “api” values
processingTypes. Refer to the API specs for details on how to configure
each specific mission field and various options for Mission processing.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Mission data from servers or the cloud</span>*

If you have your own server- or cloud-based orchestrator or scheduler,
you can call the Missions API directly via REST calls.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Missions controlled by human operators</span>*

If there is no centralized controller and the robot is directed by
individual operators, your system can make direct calls to the API from
a remote device, such as a mobile device or laptop, for instance based
on a button selection by an operator on that device.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Missions Data Model</span>

The Mission data model consists of “Mission” objects. They have a few
basic properties, a number of optional properties, and placeholders for
user-provided data. This data can be fed explicitly through APIs (e.g.,
via JSON objects), or inferred by the mission tracking service, as
described below.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Schema</span>*

The Mission object structure is captured in the [Mission Tracking
API](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#tag/missions) document, with
details on
[creating](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/createMission)
and
[updating](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/updateMission)
the mission along with the overall schema. Additional calls are
available for
[querying](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/findMissions)
(with filters),
[terminating](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/terminateMission),
and
[retrieving](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/getMission)
information about missions.

The object is broken down as follows:

- <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">missionId</span>:
  a unique identifier for the mission
- <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">robotId</span>:
  the robot running (or to run) this mission
- Timestamps:
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">startTs</span>,
    <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">endTs</span>:
    correspond to the mission start and end time. Any of them can be
    missing. For example when a mission is created and not running (as
    scheduled), they are not set.
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">scheduledTs</span>:
    to set a planned execution timestamp.
  - updatedTs: a last-update timestamp, set by the system.
- State/Status:
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">inProgress</span>:
    a boolean value for if the mission is currently executing. 
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">state</span>:
    a string representing the state of the mission. See below for
    built-in states. 
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">status</span>:
    one of the platform statuses: Ok, Warning or Error. It is used as an
    overall flag of the mission status. Related to the robot status in
    terms of values and colors used throughout the platform 
  - When updating a
    <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">state</span>,
    the services can guess the
    <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">status</span>
    and
    <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">inProgress</span>
    flags of the mission.
  - Color codings are available to represent mission
    <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">status
    (+state)</span> flags.

<!-- -->

- Contents and progress:
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">completedPercent</span>:
    An estimated percentage of completion. This can be set by a user
    invoking the APIs, as well as inferred  by the system. For example
    if there are 4 tasks and the user sets currentTaskId to the second
    one, a value 25 is guessed. If the tasks each contain an
    estimatedDurationSecs, the completion percentage calculation is more
    accurate.
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">estimatedDurationSecs</span>:
    User-set estimated duration (or: calculated by the system as the sum
    of tasks’ estimatedDurationSecs).
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">currentTaskId</span>:
    The task currently in execution
- Mission Metadata
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">arguments</span>:
    free-form object with mission parameters, e.g. route id, room id,
    package ids, etc.
  - <span style="font-size: 10px; background-color: #efefef;">data</span>:
    (reserved for collecting mission data during the run)

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">State and Statuses</span>*

The built-in mission states that are supported include:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
| **<span style="font-size: 11px;">State</span>** | **<span style="font-size: 11px;">Status (default)</span>** | **<span style="font-size: 11px;">Description</span>** |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Scheduled</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #3c78d8;">OK</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px;">Scheduled but not started</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">In progress</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #3c78d8;">OK</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px;">Currently executing</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Completed</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #3c78d8;">OK</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px;">Successfully completed (can also have custom semantics)</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Cancelled</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #ff0000;">ERR</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Intentionally cancelled</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Abandoned</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #ff0000;">ERR</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Cancelled due to an error</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Lost</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #ff0000;">ERR</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Robot lost (mislocalized)</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Paused</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #f6b26b;">WARN</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Paused (not necessarily late yet)</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Late</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #f6b26b;">WARN</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Didn’t start on time or late beyond a threshold</span> |
| <span style="font-size: 10px;">Stale</span> | <span style="font-size: 10px; color: #f6b26b;">WARN</span> | <span style="font-size: 11px;">Latest data exceeds a time threshold since update</span> |

Any number of user-defined states can be used. They can be associated
with any status. See [Mission
Tracking](https://developer.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#configuring-mission-tracking)
configuration.

In ROS environments, it is also common to use
[ActionLib](http://wiki.ros.org/actionlib) and report [goal
statuses](http://docs.ros.org/en/kinetic/api/actionlib_msgs/html/msg/GoalStatus.html).
These can also be mapped to mission states. Contact us at at
[support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai) for more details.

##### <span style="font-size: 13px;">Mission Dashboard</span>

The Missions dashboard is the primary place to explore missions in
InOrbit Control. By default, this dashboard consists of 3 sections.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Mission KPIs</span>*

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png"
style="width: 640px; margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; display: block;"
loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=320&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 320w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 640w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=960&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 960w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=1280&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 1280w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=1600&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_widget.png?width=1920&amp;name=mission_kpis_widget.png 1920w"
sizes="(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px" width="640"
alt="mission_kpis_widget" />

KPIs (Key Performance Indicators) provide a glanceable view of the
real-time data that most matters to you about your selected set of
missions. Sparklines are included in the display to provide appropriate
recent trends for each KPI. InOrbit Control includes the following
Mission KPIs by default:

- Mission success rate (%)
- Mission frequency (e.g., per day per robot)
- Average mission duration (hours:minutes:seconds)
- Average mission distance (e.g., meters)
- Average incidents per mission

KPIs also include an aggregate count of the selected set (e.g., missions
and robots, as appropriate) included in the overall set, or in
individual groups when selecting a grouping category. As mentioned
above, this also enables very efficient comparison of sets of missions
across the KPIs that are active.

<span style="text-decoration: underline;">The KPI set is fully
extensible to automate any KPIs that our customers value</span> (e.g.,
specific number of missions of different types, coverage, uptime), as
long as InOrbit has access to that data for processing. Please contact
us at [support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai) for any specific
KPIs you would like to enable for your application, as we are happy to
assist our customers with the process of customizing these.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Mission Control widget</span>*

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png"
style="width: 640px; margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; display: block;"
loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=320&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 320w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 640w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=960&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 960w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=1280&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 1280w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=1600&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_control_widget.png?width=1920&amp;name=mission_control_widget.png 1920w"
sizes="(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px" width="640"
alt="mission_control_widget" />

As with the Fleet and Robot dashboards, there is a Mission Control
widget for missions. This widget enables selecting sets of missions to
focus on throughout the rest of the dashboard, with options for
grouping, along with several types of filtering.

Grouping enables simple comparison of the aggregate data for different
sets of missions (e.g., Location, Robot Type) for direct comparison.
Filters are available for combinations of arbitrary sets of robots,
tags, or mission states. There are also selectors for time and date
range to easily go back a specific number of hours, days, or months or
only include missions for a given date range.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png"
style="width: 640px; margin-left: auto; margin-right: auto; display: block;"
loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=320&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 320w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=640&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 640w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=960&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 960w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=1280&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 1280w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=1600&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 1600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/Mission%20tracking/mission_kpis_grouping.png?width=1920&amp;name=mission_kpis_grouping.png 1920w"
sizes="(max-width: 640px) 100vw, 640px" width="640"
alt="mission_kpis_grouping" />

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Mission Details table</span>*

The Mission Details table provides high level detail on the individual
missions that comprise the selected set. The table provides real-time
and historic State information along with other basic data around the
mission, including the Robot and Start and End time. Every mission can
be expanded to see the data values that you select that best represents
that mission for your purposes, along with a button to activate Time
Capsule for that mission for deeper review of how it went and all of the
critical time-series data associated with it.

##### <span style="color: #666666;">Examples</span>

While we have been focusing on the interactive selection and
presentation of missions, here are some basic examples of using the API
to access mission data and inform the mission tracking.

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">REST API calls</span>*

In this first example, we mark the start of a mission, send a mission
update and complete it using REST API calls. 

First, we call the
[createMission](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/createMission)
API for a robot with id <span style="font-size: 10px;">ID1234</span> and
some basic data including a mission payload field
<span style="font-size: 10px;">direction</span>.

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`curl --location --request POST `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`'https://api.inorbit.ai/missions'`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">` \`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`--header `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`'Content-Type: application/json'`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">` \`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`--header `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`'x-auth-inorbit-app-key: YOUR-APP-KEY`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">` \`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`--data-raw `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`'{`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`   `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"robotId"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`: `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"ID1234"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`,`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`   `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"label"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`: `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"Aisles 123-456"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`,`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`   `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"arguments"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`: {`</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`       `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"direction"`</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">`: `</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`"North"`</span>

```
   }
```

 

<span style="font-size: 9px;">`}`</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #2a00ff;">`'`</span>

**<span style="background-color: #ffff00;">Note:</span>**<span style="background-color: #ffff00;">
In this example we used CURL to make explicit every HTTP header and
payload that gets sent. We recommend testing the APIs with a similar
tool or through a testing tool (e.g. Postman), although a production
system will call these APIs programmatically. In the following examples
we will omit such details and focus on the JSON payloads only.</span>

The call above creates a mission. The API call receives back a JSON
response with the mission data. Note that several fields are derived (in
this case a <span style="font-size: 10px;">state</span>, several
timestamps, and a <span style="font-size: 10px;">missionId</span>).

<span style="font-size: 9px;">{</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"robotId"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"gont-noetic"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"label"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"Aisles
123-456"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"arguments"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
{</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">       </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"direction"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"North"</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   },</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"inProgress"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">false</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"state"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"scheduled"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"status"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"OK"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"createdTs"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #098658;">1640173104711</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"updatedTs"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #098658;">1640173104711</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"scheduledTs"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #098658;">1640173104711</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"missionId"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"37049929-8e15-4419-8a0d-2c14e3b5a90c"</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">}</span>

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=539&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png"
style="width: 539px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=270&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 270w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=539&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 539w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=809&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 809w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=1078&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 1078w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=1348&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 1348w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png?width=1617&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-04-27%20(1).png 1617w"
sizes="(max-width: 539px) 100vw, 539px" width="539"

This mission was created as “scheduled”, since no explicit state flag
was set, and the inProgress flag was not set either. This could be a
call from a mission planner or scheduler, pre-creating the mission. Next
we will simulate starting it, this time calling the
[updateMission](https://api.inorbit.ai/docs/index.html#operation/updateMission)
API (via HTTP PUT), with this payload:

<span style="font-size: 9px;">{</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"inProgress"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">true</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"completedPercent"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #098658;">0.66</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">}</span>

The API response includes the updated mission object with the new field
values (and updated timestamps), omitted here for brevity.

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=535&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png"
style="width: 535px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=268&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 268w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=535&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 535w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=803&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 803w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=1070&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 1070w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=1338&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 1338w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png?width=1605&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-23%20(1).png 1605w"
sizes="(max-width: 535px) 100vw, 535px" width="535"
alt="Screenshot from 2022-08-19 16-05-23 (1)" />

Finally, we end the mission by setting the inProgress flag, and giving
it a new state.

<span style="font-size: 9px;">{</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"inProgress"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">true</span>**<span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"state"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #0451a5;">"completed"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">,</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">   </span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #a31515;">"completedPercent"</span><span style="font-size: 9px;">:
</span><span style="font-size: 9px; color: #098658;">1.0</span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">  </span>

<span style="font-size: 9px;">}</span>

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=538&amp;height=248&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png"
style="width: 538px;" loading="lazy"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=269&amp;height=124&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 269w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=538&amp;height=248&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 538w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=807&amp;height=372&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 807w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=1076&amp;height=496&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 1076w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=1345&amp;height=620&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 1345w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png?width=1614&amp;height=744&amp;name=Screenshot%20from%202022-08-19%2016-05-43%20(1).png 1614w"
sizes="(max-width: 538px) 100vw, 538px" width="538" height="248"
alt="Screenshot from 2022-08-19 16-05-43 (1)" />

###### *<span style="color: #666666;">Dynamic Updates using Data Sources</span>*

The previous example assumes a scenario where the user can set up a
component to communicate about missions to InOrbit. This can be from a
mission planner, some ETL, or any cloud component based on the customer
infrastructure.

In a different setting, the robots’ agents can be set up to propagate
mission updates, knowing that in most cases the robots are aware of
their own missions (start, end, status, mission metadata). For this
scenario, the Missions Tracking support needs to be configured to
connect some [data
sources](https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#customize-robot) to act
as mission triggers. They can include:

A data source to act as a mission “delimiter”, with specific values to
imply the start of a mission. This is normally related to
[Modes](https://www.inorbit.ai/docs?hsLang=en#collections-locations-modes).
For example a key-value data source such as
“<span style="font-size: 10px;">mode=delivery</span>” can trigger the
start of a mission.

Other data sources can be mapped to the mission
<span style="font-size: 10px;">label</span>, the
<span style="font-size: 10px;">status</span> and
<span style="font-size: 10px;">state</span> flags, or any payload in the
<span style="font-size: 10px;">data</span> field.

In this scenario, the same example as above could be accomplished via
the following sequence (assuming a ROS agent publishing to InOrbit’s
data sources topic):

- Publish<span style="font-size: 10px;"> mission=delivery</span>  (At
  this point the mission is already marked as started, with no further
  data)
- Publish <span style="font-size: 10px;">mission-target=Aisles
  123-456</span> (sets the mission label)
- Publish <span style="font-size: 10px;">scan-completion=0.66</span>
  (sets some progress)
- …
- Publish<span style="font-size: 10px;"> mission=docking</span> (Ends
  the mission)

The advantage of this scenario is simplicity of implementation: It only
requires that the robot implementation can broadcast mission-related
information (for example with a
[republisher](https://github.com/inorbit-ai/ros_inorbit_samples/tree/noetic-devel/inorbit_republisher)),
and takes advantage of the secure, optimized data connection between the
InOrbit agent and the cloud.

###### Advanced Configuration

As explained, there are several ways to configure and use InOrbit
Mission Tracking. There are even more variants not covered in this
document, including the possibility to time out missions after
inactivity, selecting default values for some fields and preventing
fields from overwriting each other when multiple concurrent updates
happen. 

If you are missing any feature around Missions Tracking, chances are
that the InOrbit team can configure them for you. Do not hesitate to
contact us at at [support@inorbit.ai](mailto:support@inorbit.ai) for
help.

</div>

</div>

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

InOrbit supports 2-way synchronization with
<a href="https://www.atlassian.com/software/opsgenie" rel="noopener"
target="_blank">Opsgenie</a>, a platform for on-call and alert
management used by DevOps teams. Through this premium integration:

- Incidents in InOrbit trigger alerts in Opsgenie
- Resolving incidents in InOrbit resolves the associated alerts in
  Opsgenie.
- Alerts created in Opsgenie can trigger incidents in InOrbit.
- Closing an alert originated in Opsgenie, resolves the incident in
  InOrbit.

In all cases, alerts in Opsgenie are enriched with data from InOrbit,
including the latest events from the audit log, robot status and
collections.

##### **Enabling the integration**:

1.  **Create API key in Opsgenie**
2.  In Opsgenie go the the integrations list and add Rest API HTTPS over
    JSON. Copy the generated API key.  
    <img
    src="https://lh5.googleusercontent.com/Wbgi58zK2CjMYIJ4y-owLS6ChZLUpbpZKd2iToK9BBjiGgC7wZp8PVVPBITSNIefzGbzdthk2zaQK-l691MFDpFzaOgFw9gmgnPsw0CBQ3L0uA_2TglcYXHq2KQNu6OZee_nfNLu"
    style="width: 600px;" width="600" />  
      
3.  **Configure Opsgenie integration**  
    As administrator, login to <a href="https://control.inorbit.ai/"
    style="color: #3b3fb1;"><span>InOrbit Control</span></a>; in the
    upper right, click Settings in the 3 vertical dots menu, select the
    Admin tab and on the left menu click on integrations. Turn on
    Opsgenie and enter the API key from Step
    <span style="font-size: 11pt; color: #000000;"><img
    src="https://lh4.googleusercontent.com/nkkgQD0stpgCTQHZarhKvw6yQqnBatx0uZ-QWs22HX6KrT6iNXC4gYo5wKvBKNDvxK6hddYFTw7dJaAH64C_PD_Q-bB4fvXXWzLszc4jjd6FD94XzBi9hKyTKEqCxp34-R1enlLf"
    style="width: 600px;" width="600" /></span>
4.  **Configure Opsgenie webhook**   
    1.  In Opsgenie go the the integrations list and add Webhook API
    2.  Configure a custom header “x-opsgenie-webhook-token” using the
        value shown in InOrbit.  
          
        <img
        src="https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/21EEl4FNu3Iw-GxMC4Ubdu32KITmL4lCLfvizOkLjlFFXHqPokTPCXtD2vmjIeb3lxM66ayMyF1hJjnnnwxtUItgQLJcI-78wYCS340B6t-qmfXda8N_xmIqYjIW0fEK4JMfvTQK"
        style="width: 600px;" width="600" />  
          
    3.  This value can be obtained from InOrbit in the Opsgenie
        configuration section, “Opsgenie webhook token”:  
          
        <span style="font-size: 11pt; color: #000000;"><img
        src="https://lh6.googleusercontent.com/bl36NtOJLSLChsdXh8uZ5CMcyop83cTUzarhDy3Hpu4r_ozzrN8YtwClV4t3JEd9WGEnrFof1z-OhZvLsVOxihdFsrUajEX86kYhAUmsQzjpRxZgpzeErGN2R1TROC9C4eYt6R5L"
        style="width: 600px;" width="600" /></span>
    4.  Check “Add Alert Description to Payload” and “Add Alert Details
        to Payload”  
        <img
        src="https://lh4.googleusercontent.com/4RM3IUaXVNxLY9EB8Ku848SXUIwpvq04hUAIqvw5Q0n4EUuNgCyJ9zNMMUv4XpGrn-yjZhY84opA2y-W60gOKwJ3krV4AhrAvYNJb4Jduss3JjVwnxtrWlkeUV-KnTEGzNAKkJM-"
        style="width: 600px;" width="600" />  
          
    5.  Enter the Webhook URL obtained from InOrbit in the Opsgenie
        configuration section, “Opsgenie webhook URL”  
          
        <img
        src="https://lh5.googleusercontent.com/Z681rD5GYKshZS3Y0btAEu1eKTSX1FbUUaijjXL1LsxrBFGk-xDLr_HTjh1wbEbINPMXY5dkfdwbogDUtrgQUzyZJ_X2xaR991nvGA3jykFubANQYcSKk09j5Rhuw4dGx8_Xqlb7"
        style="width: 600px;" width="600" />

  
  

##### **Using Opsgenie as alert channel for an incident**

Go to the incidents definitions page at Settings → Insights → Incidents.
Edit or create an incident definition and select Opsgenie as the alert
channel.

<img
src="https://lh4.googleusercontent.com/vQXcSxmPz17Ya1qBW29wBNjtmG1JeVDMvxjkAGOf8GbjhDud5aEJxMjZiGMII6N5gV4XjZHIqQS58zwmsAAn5ldDa7VZSqhEpDgXAkvwL09-4YOCm2P3J75IjsQRkJbmreMsjgMr"
style="width: 600px;" width="600" />

 

Next time the incident triggers, an alert will be created in Opsgenie.

 

##### **Triggering an InOrbit incident from an Opsgenie alert**

You can create an alert in Opsgenie specifying a robot name or id as the
Entity.

<img
src="https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/N2eef3tabCq7I6yPL_tfVoPSH9L8eN77rflTDg5Li8RyUP2dw09YtZjCE3nC8GDfEqi3-bOukNUO5yIG2Q_L8u1wI152jYMuhTI-G9WCnwcMB3ACnZl4_jr4b3CaTV40mb-1GH7y"
style="width: 600px;" width="600" />

This will automatically create an incident in InOrbit

<img
src="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=600&amp;name=incident-1.png"
style="width: 600px;"
srcset="https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=300&amp;name=incident-1.png 300w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=600&amp;name=incident-1.png 600w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=900&amp;name=incident-1.png 900w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=1200&amp;name=incident-1.png 1200w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=1500&amp;name=incident-1.png 1500w, https://www.inorbit.ai/hs-fs/hubfs/doc/NDL%20Images/Docs/HOWTO%20Incident%20management%20-%20incident%20definitions/incident-1.png?width=1800&amp;name=incident-1.png 1800w"
sizes="(max-width: 600px) 100vw, 600px" width="600" alt="incident-1" />

<div role="main">

<div class="hs-accordion__item-content">

<span style="font-size: 10.5pt; color: #807575;">InOrbit supports
integration to any incident management platform or internal systems via
webhooks and a REST API.</span>

<span style="font-size: 10.5pt; color: #807575;">In order to receive
events from InOrbit, your incident management system must provide a
webhook. InOrbit then sends events to your webhook according to what you
have configured, including when an incident is triggered. Webhook events
are sent by InOrbit as POST requests to your webhook.</span>

##### <span style="font-size: 14pt; color: #434343;">Setting up your Webhook</span>

<span style="font-size: 14pt; color: #434343;"><img
src="https://lh5.googleusercontent.com/AWShl98Gp45uSZ4Qyp5HeuDkYO3Y8JeO4byq24cJiE9FWE5yBipp6f533BOKYeUEVCLvzoUP9DbopRDumtGjVUUAgm9O1HqaLQGDacJLhe-Q8c8uL3xIynW45da5NTkipkR4KHo-"
style="width: 600px;" width="600" /></span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">Setting up a callback URL
for webhook events requires verification that you control or own the
domain that hosts your application.</span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">When you provide the
webhook URL in Settings \> Admin \>Integrations, you will be sent a GET
request including the header X-InOrbit-Key (a fixed value, randomly
generated), which will contain the API key you see on the screen. Your
server should respond with a status code of 200 and the API key that was
sent.</span>

##### <span style="font-size: 14pt; color: #434343;">Webhook Events</span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">You will receive one
webhook event per instance of an incident occurring.</span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">For example, if you
configured a Warning to be triggered when the Battery Level goes under
30%, you will receive a POST request when that happens.</span>

<span style="font-size: 14pt; color: #434343;"><span style="font-size: 16px;">`{`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  alertId: <Alert ID>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  entity: {`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`     id: <Entity ID>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`     type: <Entity Type>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`     name: <Entity Name>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  },`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  message: <Message of the alert>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  description: <Description, usually more detailed than message>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  actions: <Possible actions that can be executed for the given alert>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  componentId: <Component ID where the alert was triggered>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  status: <Status, could be "new", "resolved">,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  ts: <Timestamp when the alert was triggered>,`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`  priority: <Severity of the current alert>`</span></span>

<span style="font-size: 16px;"><span style="color: #434343;">`}`</span></span>

 

##### **<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">Incidents REST API</span>**

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">If you use an external
incident management system to receive service requests, you may also
trigger a corresponding incident within InOrbit by using the REST API.
Incidents created and resolved using the API will be shown in
InOrbit.</span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">To enable the API, go to
Settings \> Admin \> Integrations and turn on the toggle.</span>

 

<span style="font-size: 11pt; color: #000000;"><img
src="https://lh4.googleusercontent.com/b6FUolKvVszeBJpRvUUH1kpLsgYO0LBbXkF76hnCx50anLmpfnvgxUzOvkrK7Y9fdsP5wRUFDPumN-fZLoBU7KiTDw4o4rIEUddB7Gwdcrf0bIsJWVrhTGflhmS8BVFbL5lSWuID"
style="width: 600px;" width="600" /></span>

<span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">You can find the API
specification at</span>
<a href="https://api.alpha.inorbit.ai/docs/robots.html"
style="font-size: 11pt;"><span>https://api.alpha.inorbit.ai/docs/robots.html</span></a><span style="font-size: 12pt; color: #807575;">.
To obtain your API Key, select the green Authorize button.</span>

</div>

</div>
